[](undefined)  350   /*
[](undefined)  351    * This part deals with the special case where a unit wants to inline xxHash,
[](undefined)  352    * but "xxhash.h" has previously been included without XXH_INLINE_ALL,
[](undefined)  353    * such as part of some previously included *.h header file.
[](undefined)  354    * Without further action, the new include would just be ignored,
[](undefined)  355    * and functions would effectively _not_ be inlined (silent failure).
[](undefined)  356    * The following macros solve this situation by prefixing all inlined names,
[](undefined)  357    * avoiding naming collision with previous inclusions.
[](undefined)  358    */
[](undefined)  359   /* Before that, we unconditionally #undef all symbols,
[](undefined)  360    * in case they were already defined with XXH_NAMESPACE.
[](undefined)  361    * They will then be redefined for XXH_INLINE_ALL
[](undefined)  362    */
[](undefined)  363#  undef XXH_versionNumber

[](undefined) 1448/*******   Streaming   *******/
[](undefined) 1449#ifndef XXH_NO_STREAM
[](undefined) 1450/*
[](undefined) 1451 * Streaming requires state maintenance.
[](undefined) 1452 * This operation costs memory and CPU.
[](undefined) 1453 * As a consequence, streaming is slower than one-shot hashing.
[](undefined) 1454 * For better performance, prefer one-shot functions whenever applicable.
[](undefined) 1455 *
[](undefined) 1456 * XXH3_128bits uses the same XXH3_state_t as XXH3_64bits().
[](undefined) 1457 * Use already declared XXH3_createState() and XXH3_freeState().
[](undefined) 1458 *
[](undefined) 1459 * All reset and streaming functions have same meaning as their 64-bit counterpart.
[](undefined) 1460 */

[](undefined) 2065/*-**********************************************************************
[](undefined) 2066 * xxHash implementation
[](undefined) 2067 *-**********************************************************************
[](undefined) 2068 * xxHash's implementation used to be hosted inside xxhash.c.
[](undefined) 2069 *
[](undefined) 2070 * However, inlining requires implementation to be visible to the compiler,
[](undefined) 2071 * hence be included alongside the header.
[](undefined) 2072 * Previously, implementation was hosted inside xxhash.c,
[](undefined) 2073 * which was then #included when inlining was activated.
[](undefined) 2074 * This construction created issues with a few build and install systems,
[](undefined) 2075 * as it required xxhash.c to be stored in /include directory.
[](undefined) 2076 *
[](undefined) 2077 * xxHash implementation is now directly integrated within xxhash.h.
[](undefined) 2078 * As a consequence, xxhash.c is no longer needed in /include.
[](undefined) 2079 *
[](undefined) 2080 * xxhash.c is still available and is still useful.
[](undefined) 2081 * In a "normal" setup, when xxhash is not inlined,
[](undefined) 2082 * xxhash.h only exposes the prototypes and public symbols,
[](undefined) 2083 * while xxhash.c can be built into an object file xxhash.o
[](undefined) 2084 * which can then be linked into the final binary.
[](undefined) 2085 ************************************************************************/
[](undefined) 2086

[](undefined) 2634#if (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==3))
[](undefined) 2635/*
[](undefined) 2636 * Manual byteshift. Best for old compilers which don't inline memcpy.
[](undefined) 2637 * We actually directly use XXH_readLE32 and XXH_readBE32.
[](undefined) 2638 */
[](undefined) 2639#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==2))
[](undefined) 2640
[](undefined) 2641/*
[](undefined) 2642 * Force direct memory access. Only works on CPU which support unaligned memory
[](undefined) 2643 * access in hardware.
[](undefined) 2644 */
[](undefined) 2645static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* memPtr) { return *(const xxh_u32*) memPtr; }
[](undefined) 2646
[](undefined) 2647#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==1))
[](undefined) 2648
[](undefined) 2649/*
[](undefined) 2650 * __attribute__((aligned(1))) is supported by gcc and clang. Originally the
[](undefined) 2651 * documentation claimed that it only increased the alignment, but actually it
[](undefined) 2652 * can decrease it on gcc, clang, and icc:
[](undefined) 2653 * https://gcc.gnu.org/bugzilla/show_bug.cgi?id=69502,
[](undefined) 2654 * https://gcc.godbolt.org/z/xYez1j67Y.
[](undefined) 2655 */
[](undefined) 2656#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 2657typedef union { xxh_u32 u32; } __attribute__((__packed__)) unalign;
[](undefined) 2658#endif
[](undefined) 2659static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* ptr)
[](undefined) 2660{
[](undefined) 2661    typedef __attribute__((__aligned__(1))) __attribute__((__may_alias__)) xxh_u32 xxh_unalign32;
[](undefined) 2662    return *((const xxh_unalign32*)ptr);
[](undefined) 2663}
[](undefined) 2664
[](undefined) 2665#else
[](undefined) 2666
[](undefined) 2667/*
[](undefined) 2668 * Portable and safe solution. Generally efficient.
[](undefined) 2669 * see: https://fastcompression.blogspot.com/2015/08/accessing-unaligned-memory.html
[](undefined) 2670 */
[](undefined) 2671static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 2672{
[](undefined) 2673    xxh_u32 val;
[](undefined) 2674    XXH_memcpy(&val, memPtr, sizeof(val));
[](undefined) 2675    return val;
[](undefined) 2676}
[](undefined) 2677
[](undefined) 2678#endif   /* XXH_FORCE_DIRECT_MEMORY_ACCESS */

[](undefined) 2701 * Try to detect endianness automatically, to avoid the nonstandard behavior
[](undefined) 2702 * in `XXH_isLittleEndian()`
[](undefined) 2703 */
[](undefined) 2704#  if defined(_WIN32) /* Windows is always little endian */ \
[](undefined) 2705     || defined(__LITTLE_ENDIAN__) \
[](undefined) 2706     || (defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && __BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_LITTLE_ENDIAN__)
[](undefined) 2707#    define XXH_CPU_LITTLE_ENDIAN 1
[](undefined) 2708#  elif defined(__BIG_ENDIAN__) \
[](undefined) 2709     || (defined(__BYTE_ORDER__) && __BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__)
[](undefined) 2710#    define XXH_CPU_LITTLE_ENDIAN 0
[](undefined) 2711#  else
[](undefined) 2718static int XXH_isLittleEndian(void)
[](undefined) 2719{
[](undefined) 2720    /*
[](undefined) 2721     * Portable and well-defined behavior.
[](undefined) 2722     * Don't use static: it is detrimental to performance.
[](undefined) 2723     */
[](undefined) 2724    const union { xxh_u32 u; xxh_u8 c[4]; } one = { 1 };
[](undefined) 2725    return one.c[0];
[](undefined) 2726}

[](undefined) 2747/*
[](undefined) 2748 * C23 and future versions have standard "unreachable()".
[](undefined) 2749 * Once it has been implemented reliably we can add it as an
[](undefined) 2750 * additional case:
[](undefined) 2751 *
[](undefined) 2752 * ```
[](undefined) 2753 * #if defined(__STDC_VERSION__) && (__STDC_VERSION__ >= 202311L)
[](undefined) 2754 * #  include <stddef.h>
[](undefined) 2755 * #  ifdef unreachable
[](undefined) 2756 * #    define XXH_UNREACHABLE() unreachable()
[](undefined) 2757 * #  endif
[](undefined) 2758 * #endif
[](undefined) 2759 * ```
[](undefined) 2760 *
[](undefined) 2761 * Note C++23 also has std::unreachable() which can be detected
[](undefined) 2762 * as follows:
[](undefined) 2763 * ```
[](undefined) 2764 * #if defined(__cpp_lib_unreachable) && (__cpp_lib_unreachable >= 202202L)
[](undefined) 2765 * #  include <utility>
[](undefined) 2766 * #  define XXH_UNREACHABLE() std::unreachable()
[](undefined) 2767 * #endif
[](undefined) 2768 * ```
[](undefined) 2769 * NB: `__cpp_lib_unreachable` is defined in the `<version>` header.
[](undefined) 2770 * We don't use that as including `<utility>` in `extern "C"` blocks
[](undefined) 2771 * doesn't work on GCC12
[](undefined) 2772 */
[](undefined) 2773
[](undefined) 2774#if XXH_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_unreachable)
[](undefined) 2775#  define XXH_UNREACHABLE() __builtin_unreachable()

[](undefined) 2947static xxh_u32 XXH32_round(xxh_u32 acc, xxh_u32 input)
[](undefined) 2948{
[](undefined) 2949    acc += input * [XXH_PRIME32_2](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79);
[](undefined) 2950    acc  = XXH_rotl32(acc, 13);
[](undefined) 2951    acc *= [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f);
[](undefined) 2952#if (defined(__SSE4_1__) || defined(__aarch64__) || defined(__wasm_simd128__)) && !defined(XXH_ENABLE_AUTOVECTORIZE)
[](undefined) 2953    /*
[](undefined) 2954     * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 2955     * A compiler fence is used to prevent GCC and Clang from
[](undefined) 2956     * autovectorizing the XXH32 loop (pragmas and attributes don't work for some
[](undefined) 2957     * reason) without globally disabling SSE4.1.
[](undefined) 2958     *
[](undefined) 2959     * The reason we want to avoid vectorization is because despite working on
[](undefined) 2960     * 4 integers at a time, there are multiple factors slowing XXH32 down on
[](undefined) 2961     * SSE4:
[](undefined) 2962     * - There's a ridiculous amount of lag from pmulld (10 cycles of latency on
[](undefined) 2963     *   newer chips!) making it slightly slower to multiply four integers at
[](undefined) 2964     *   once compared to four integers independently. Even when pmulld was
[](undefined) 2965     *   fastest, Sandy/Ivy Bridge, it is still not worth it to go into SSE
[](undefined) 2966     *   just to multiply unless doing a long operation.
[](undefined) 2967     *
[](undefined) 2968     * - Four instructions are required to rotate,
[](undefined) 2969     *      movqda tmp,  v // not required with VEX encoding
[](undefined) 2970     *      pslld  tmp, 13 // tmp <<= 13
[](undefined) 2971     *      psrld  v,   19 // x >>= 19
[](undefined) 2972     *      por    v,  tmp // x |= tmp
[](undefined) 2973     *   compared to one for scalar:
[](undefined) 2974     *      roll   v, 13    // reliably fast across the board
[](undefined) 2975     *      shldl  v, v, 13 // Sandy Bridge and later prefer this for some reason
[](undefined) 2976     *
[](undefined) 2977     * - Instruction level parallelism is actually more beneficial here because
[](undefined) 2978     *   the SIMD actually serializes this operation: While v1 is rotating, v2
[](undefined) 2979     *   can load data, while v3 can multiply. SSE forces them to operate
[](undefined) 2980     *   together.
[](undefined) 2981     *
[](undefined) 2982     * This is also enabled on AArch64, as Clang is *very aggressive* in vectorizing
[](undefined) 2983     * the loop. NEON is only faster on the A53, and with the newer cores, it is less
[](undefined) 2984     * than half the speed.
[](undefined) 2985     *
[](undefined) 2986     * Additionally, this is used on WASM SIMD128 because it JITs to the same
[](undefined) 2987     * SIMD instructions and has the same issue.
[](undefined) 2988     */
[](undefined) 2989    XXH_COMPILER_GUARD(acc);
[](undefined) 2990#endif
[](undefined) 2991    return acc;
[](undefined) 2992}
[](undefined) 2993
[](undefined) 3004static xxh_u32 XXH32_avalanche(xxh_u32 hash)
[](undefined) 3005{
[](undefined) 3006    hash ^= hash >> 15;
[](undefined) 3007    hash *= [XXH_PRIME32_2](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79);
[](undefined) 3008    hash ^= hash >> 13;
[](undefined) 3009    hash *= [XXH_PRIME32_3](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#gacdda6ef95561d39cf2f9b7f2cd5c6693);
[](undefined) 3010    hash ^= hash >> 16;
[](undefined) 3011    return hash;
[](undefined) 3012}

[](undefined) 3036XXH_FORCE_INLINE const xxh_u8 *
[](undefined) 3037XXH32_consumeLong(

[](undefined) 3039    xxh_u8 const *XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 3040    size_t len,
[](undefined) 3041    [XXH_alignment](group__impl.html#gadec56fccd4f83e34902b6b09ecccfff5) align

[](undefined) 3043{
[](undefined) 3044    const xxh_u8* const bEnd = input + len;
[](undefined) 3045    const xxh_u8* const limit = bEnd - 15;
[](undefined) 3046    XXH_ASSERT(acc != NULL);
[](undefined) 3047    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 3048    XXH_ASSERT(len >= 16);
[](undefined) 3049    do {
[](undefined) 3050        acc[0] = XXH32_round(acc[0], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;
[](undefined) 3051        acc[1] = XXH32_round(acc[1], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;

[](undefined) 3053        acc[3] = XXH32_round(acc[3], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;

[](undefined) 3055
[](undefined) 3056    return input;

[](undefined) 3357 * Manual byteshift. Best for old compilers which don't inline memcpy.

[](undefined) 3359 */
[](undefined) 3360#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==2))
[](undefined) 3361
[](undefined) 3362/* Force direct memory access. Only works on CPU which support unaligned memory access in hardware */
[](undefined) 3363static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3364{
[](undefined) 3365    return *(const xxh_u64*) memPtr;
[](undefined) 3366}
[](undefined) 3367
[](undefined) 3368#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==1))
[](undefined) 3369
[](undefined) 3370/*
[](undefined) 3371 * __attribute__((aligned(1))) is supported by gcc and clang. Originally the
[](undefined) 3372 * documentation claimed that it only increased the alignment, but actually it
[](undefined) 3373 * can decrease it on gcc, clang, and icc:
[](undefined) 3374 * https://gcc.gnu.org/bugzilla/show_bug.cgi?id=69502,
[](undefined) 3375 * https://gcc.godbolt.org/z/xYez1j67Y.
[](undefined) 3376 */
[](undefined) 3377#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 3378typedef union { xxh_u32 u32; xxh_u64 u64; } __attribute__((__packed__)) unalign64;
[](undefined) 3379#endif
[](undefined) 3380static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* ptr)
[](undefined) 3381{
[](undefined) 3382    typedef __attribute__((__aligned__(1))) __attribute__((__may_alias__)) xxh_u64 xxh_unalign64;
[](undefined) 3383    return *((const xxh_unalign64*)ptr);
[](undefined) 3384}
[](undefined) 3385
[](undefined) 3386#else
[](undefined) 3387
[](undefined) 3388/*
[](undefined) 3389 * Portable and safe solution. Generally efficient.
[](undefined) 3390 * see: https://fastcompression.blogspot.com/2015/08/accessing-unaligned-memory.html
[](undefined) 3391 */
[](undefined) 3392static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3393{
[](undefined) 3394    xxh_u64 val;
[](undefined) 3395    XXH_memcpy(&val, memPtr, sizeof(val));
[](undefined) 3396    return val;
[](undefined) 3397}
[](undefined) 3398
[](undefined) 3399#endif   /* XXH_FORCE_DIRECT_MEMORY_ACCESS */
[](undefined) 3400
[](undefined) 3401#if defined(_MSC_VER)     /* Visual Studio */
[](undefined) 3402#  define XXH_swap64 _byteswap_uint64

[](undefined) 3406static xxh_u64 XXH_swap64(xxh_u64 x)
[](undefined) 3407{
[](undefined) 3408    return  ((x << 56) & 0xff00000000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3409            ((x << 40) & 0x00ff000000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3410            ((x << 24) & 0x0000ff0000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3411            ((x << 8)  & 0x000000ff00000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3412            ((x >> 8)  & 0x00000000ff000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3413            ((x >> 24) & 0x0000000000ff0000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3414            ((x >> 40) & 0x000000000000ff00ULL) |
[](undefined) 3415            ((x >> 56) & 0x00000000000000ffULL);

[](undefined) 3505     * autovectorizing the XXH64 loop (pragmas and attributes don't work for some

[](undefined) 3899/*
[](undefined) 3900 * One goal of XXH3 is to make it fast on both 32-bit and 64-bit, while
[](undefined) 3901 * remaining a true 64-bit/128-bit hash function.
[](undefined) 3902 *
[](undefined) 3903 * This is done by prioritizing a subset of 64-bit operations that can be
[](undefined) 3904 * emulated without too many steps on the average 32-bit machine.
[](undefined) 3905 *
[](undefined) 3906 * For example, these two lines seem similar, and run equally fast on 64-bit:
[](undefined) 3907 *
[](undefined) 3908 *   xxh_u64 x;
[](undefined) 3909 *   x ^= (x >> 47); // good
[](undefined) 3910 *   x ^= (x >> 13); // bad
[](undefined) 3911 *
[](undefined) 3912 * However, to a 32-bit machine, there is a major difference.
[](undefined) 3913 *
[](undefined) 3914 * x ^= (x >> 47) looks like this:
[](undefined) 3915 *
[](undefined) 3916 *   x.lo ^= (x.hi >> (47 - 32));
[](undefined) 3917 *
[](undefined) 3918 * while x ^= (x >> 13) looks like this:
[](undefined) 3919 *
[](undefined) 3920 *   // note: funnel shifts are not usually cheap.
[](undefined) 3921 *   x.lo ^= (x.lo >> 13) | (x.hi << (32 - 13));
[](undefined) 3922 *   x.hi ^= (x.hi >> 13);
[](undefined) 3923 *
[](undefined) 3924 * The first one is significantly faster than the second, simply because the
[](undefined) 3925 * shift is larger than 32. This means:
[](undefined) 3926 *  - All the bits we need are in the upper 32 bits, so we can ignore the lower
[](undefined) 3927 *    32 bits in the shift.
[](undefined) 3928 *  - The shift result will always fit in the lower 32 bits, and therefore,
[](undefined) 3929 *    we can ignore the upper 32 bits in the xor.
[](undefined) 3930 *
[](undefined) 3931 * Thanks to this optimization, XXH3 only requires these features to be efficient:
[](undefined) 3932 *
[](undefined) 3933 *  - Usable unaligned access
[](undefined) 3934 *  - A 32-bit or 64-bit ALU
[](undefined) 3935 *      - If 32-bit, a decent ADC instruction
[](undefined) 3936 *  - A 32 or 64-bit multiply with a 64-bit result
[](undefined) 3937 *  - For the 128-bit variant, a decent byteswap helps short inputs.
[](undefined) 3938 *
[](undefined) 3939 * The first two are already required by XXH32, and almost all 32-bit and 64-bit
[](undefined) 3940 * platforms which can run XXH32 can run XXH3 efficiently.
[](undefined) 3941 *
[](undefined) 3942 * Thumb-1, the classic 16-bit only subset of ARM's instruction set, is one
[](undefined) 3943 * notable exception.
[](undefined) 3944 *
[](undefined) 3945 * First of all, Thumb-1 lacks support for the UMULL instruction which
[](undefined) 3946 * performs the important long multiply. This means numerous __aeabi_lmul
[](undefined) 3947 * calls.
[](undefined) 3948 *
[](undefined) 3949 * Second of all, the 8 functional registers are just not enough.
[](undefined) 3950 * Setup for __aeabi_lmul, byteshift loads, pointers, and all arithmetic need
[](undefined) 3951 * Lo registers, and this shuffling results in thousands more MOVs than A32.
[](undefined) 3952 *
[](undefined) 3953 * A32 and T32 don't have this limitation. They can access all 14 registers,
[](undefined) 3954 * do a 32->64 multiply with UMULL, and the flexible operand allowing free
[](undefined) 3955 * shifts is helpful, too.
[](undefined) 3956 *
[](undefined) 3957 * Therefore, we do a quick sanity check.
[](undefined) 3958 *
[](undefined) 3959 * If compiling Thumb-1 for a target which supports ARM instructions, we will
[](undefined) 3960 * emit a warning, as it is not a "sane" platform to compile for.
[](undefined) 3961 *
[](undefined) 3962 * Usually, if this happens, it is because of an accident and you probably need
[](undefined) 3963 * to specify -march, as you likely meant to compile for a newer architecture.
[](undefined) 3964 *
[](undefined) 3965 * Credit: large sections of the vectorial and asm source code paths
[](undefined) 3966 *         have been contributed by @easyaspi314
[](undefined) 3967 */
[](undefined) 3968#if defined(__thumb__) && !defined(__thumb2__) && defined(__ARM_ARCH_ISA_ARM)
[](undefined) 3969#   warning "XXH3 is highly inefficient without ARM or Thumb-2."
[](undefined) 3970#endif
[](undefined) 3971

[](undefined) 3973 * Vectorization detection
[](undefined) 3974 * ========================================== */

[](undefined) 4095/*
[](undefined) 4096 * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 4097 * GCC usually generates the best code with -O3 for xxHash.
[](undefined) 4098 *
[](undefined) 4099 * However, when targeting AVX2, it is overzealous in its unrolling resulting
[](undefined) 4100 * in code roughly 3/4 the speed of Clang.
[](undefined) 4101 *
[](undefined) 4102 * There are other issues, such as GCC splitting _mm256_loadu_si256 into
[](undefined) 4103 * _mm_loadu_si128 + _mm256_inserti128_si256. This is an optimization which
[](undefined) 4104 * only applies to Sandy and Ivy Bridge... which don't even support AVX2.
[](undefined) 4105 *
[](undefined) 4106 * That is why when compiling the AVX2 version, it is recommended to use either
[](undefined) 4107 *   -O2 -mavx2 -march=haswell
[](undefined) 4108 * or
[](undefined) 4109 *   -O2 -mavx2 -mno-avx256-split-unaligned-load
[](undefined) 4110 * for decent performance, or to use Clang instead.
[](undefined) 4111 *
[](undefined) 4112 * Fortunately, we can control the first one with a pragma that forces GCC into
[](undefined) 4113 * -O2, but the other one we can't control without "failed to inline always
[](undefined) 4114 * inline function due to target mismatch" warnings.
[](undefined) 4115 */
[](undefined) 4116#if XXH_VECTOR == XXH_AVX2 /* AVX2 */ \
[](undefined) 4117  && defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__) /* GCC, not Clang */ \
[](undefined) 4118  && defined(__OPTIMIZE__) && XXH_SIZE_OPT <= 0 /* respect -O0 and -Os */
[](undefined) 4119#  pragma GCC push_options
[](undefined) 4120#  pragma GCC optimize("-O2")
[](undefined) 4121#endif
[](undefined) 4122
[](undefined) 4123#if XXH_VECTOR == XXH_NEON
[](undefined) 4124
[](undefined) 4125/*
[](undefined) 4126 * UGLY HACK: While AArch64 GCC on Linux does not seem to care, on macOS, GCC -O3
[](undefined) 4127 * optimizes out the entire hashLong loop because of the aliasing violation.
[](undefined) 4128 *
[](undefined) 4129 * However, GCC is also inefficient at load-store optimization with vld1q/vst1q,
[](undefined) 4130 * so the only option is to mark it as aliasing.
[](undefined) 4131 */
[](undefined) 4132typedef uint64x2_t xxh_aliasing_uint64x2_t XXH_ALIASING;

[](undefined) 4254/* Annoyingly, these headers _may_ define three macros: `bool`, `vector`,
[](undefined) 4255 * and `pixel`. This is a problem for obvious reasons.
[](undefined) 4256 *
[](undefined) 4257 * These keywords are unnecessary; the spec literally says they are
[](undefined) 4258 * equivalent to `__bool`, `__vector`, and `__pixel` and may be undef'd
[](undefined) 4259 * after including the header.
[](undefined) 4260 *
[](undefined) 4261 * We use pragma push_macro/pop_macro to keep the namespace clean. */
[](undefined) 4262#  pragma push_macro("bool")
[](undefined) 4263#  pragma push_macro("vector")
[](undefined) 4264#  pragma push_macro("pixel")
[](undefined) 4265/* silence potential macro redefined warnings */
[](undefined) 4266#  undef bool
[](undefined) 4267#  undef vector
[](undefined) 4268#  undef pixel
[](undefined) 4269
[](undefined) 4270#  if defined(__s390x__)

[](undefined) 4272#  else
[](undefined) 4273#    include <altivec.h>
[](undefined) 4274#  endif
[](undefined) 4275
[](undefined) 4276/* Restore the original macro values, if applicable. */
[](undefined) 4277#  pragma pop_macro("pixel")
[](undefined) 4278#  pragma pop_macro("vector")
[](undefined) 4279#  pragma pop_macro("bool")

[](undefined) 4331/*
[](undefined) 4332 * vec_mulo and vec_mule are very problematic intrinsics on PowerPC
[](undefined) 4333 *
[](undefined) 4334 * These intrinsics weren't added until GCC 8, despite existing for a while,
[](undefined) 4335 * and they are endian dependent. Also, their meaning swap depending on version.
[](undefined) 4336 * */
[](undefined) 4337# if defined(__s390x__)
[](undefined) 4338 /* s390x is always big endian, no issue on this platform */
[](undefined) 4339#  define XXH_vec_mulo vec_mulo
[](undefined) 4340#  define XXH_vec_mule vec_mule
[](undefined) 4341# elif defined(__clang__) && XXH_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_altivec_vmuleuw) && !defined(__ibmxl__)
[](undefined) 4342/* Clang has a better way to control this, we can just use the builtin which doesn't swap. */
[](undefined) 4343 /* The IBM XL Compiler (which defined __clang__) only implements the vec_* operations */
[](undefined) 4344#  define XXH_vec_mulo __builtin_altivec_vmulouw
[](undefined) 4345#  define XXH_vec_mule __builtin_altivec_vmuleuw
[](undefined) 4346# else
[](undefined) 4347/* gcc needs inline assembly */
[](undefined) 4348/* Adapted from https://github.com/google/highwayhash/blob/master/highwayhash/hh_vsx.h. */
[](undefined) 4349XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64x2 XXH_vec_mulo(xxh_u32x4 a, xxh_u32x4 b)
[](undefined) 4350{
[](undefined) 4351    xxh_u64x2 result;
[](undefined) 4352    __asm__("vmulouw %0, %1, %2" : "=v" (result) : "v" (a), "v" (b));
[](undefined) 4353    return result;
[](undefined) 4354}
[](undefined) 4355XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64x2 XXH_vec_mule(xxh_u32x4 a, xxh_u32x4 b)
[](undefined) 4356{
[](undefined) 4357    xxh_u64x2 result;
[](undefined) 4358    __asm__("vmuleuw %0, %1, %2" : "=v" (result) : "v" (a), "v" (b));
[](undefined) 4359    return result;
[](undefined) 4360}
[](undefined) 4361# endif /* XXH_vec_mulo, XXH_vec_mule */
[](undefined) 4362#endif /* XXH_VECTOR == XXH_VSX */

[](undefined) 4432#ifdef XXH_DOXYGEN
[](undefined) 4449XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64
[](undefined) 4450XXH_mult32to64(xxh_u64 x, xxh_u64 y)
[](undefined) 4451{
[](undefined) 4452   return (x & 0xFFFFFFFF) * (y & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4453}
[](undefined) 4454#elif defined(_MSC_VER) && defined(_M_IX86)
[](undefined) 4455#    define XXH_mult32to64(x, y) __emulu((unsigned)(x), (unsigned)(y))
[](undefined) 4456#else
[](undefined) 4457/*
[](undefined) 4458 * Downcast + upcast is usually better than masking on older compilers like
[](undefined) 4459 * GCC 4.2 (especially 32-bit ones), all without affecting newer compilers.
[](undefined) 4460 *
[](undefined) 4461 * The other method, (x & 0xFFFFFFFF) * (y & 0xFFFFFFFF), will AND both operands
[](undefined) 4462 * and perform a full 64x64 multiply -- entirely redundant on 32-bit.
[](undefined) 4463 */
[](undefined) 4464#    define XXH_mult32to64(x, y) ((xxh_u64)(xxh_u32)(x) * (xxh_u64)(xxh_u32)(y))
[](undefined) 4465#endif
[](undefined) 4466
[](undefined) 4476static [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html)
[](undefined) 4477XXH_mult64to128(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs)
[](undefined) 4478{
[](undefined) 4479    /*
[](undefined) 4480     * GCC/Clang __uint128_t method.
[](undefined) 4481     *
[](undefined) 4482     * On most 64-bit targets, GCC and Clang define a __uint128_t type.
[](undefined) 4483     * This is usually the best way as it usually uses a native long 64-bit
[](undefined) 4484     * multiply, such as MULQ on x86_64 or MUL + UMULH on aarch64.
[](undefined) 4485     *
[](undefined) 4486     * Usually.
[](undefined) 4487     *
[](undefined) 4488     * Despite being a 32-bit platform, Clang (and emscripten) define this type
[](undefined) 4489     * despite not having the arithmetic for it. This results in a laggy
[](undefined) 4490     * compiler builtin call which calculates a full 128-bit multiply.
[](undefined) 4491     * In that case it is best to use the portable one.
[](undefined) 4492     * https://github.com/Cyan4973/xxHash/issues/211#issuecomment-515575677
[](undefined) 4493     */
[](undefined) 4494#if (defined(__GNUC__) || defined(__clang__)) && !defined(__wasm__) \
[](undefined) 4495    && defined(__SIZEOF_INT128__) \
[](undefined) 4496    || (defined(_INTEGRAL_MAX_BITS) && _INTEGRAL_MAX_BITS >= 128)
[](undefined) 4497
[](undefined) 4498    __uint128_t const product = (__uint128_t)lhs * (__uint128_t)rhs;
[](undefined) 4499    [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) r128;
[](undefined) 4500    r128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = (xxh_u64)(product);
[](undefined) 4501    r128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = (xxh_u64)(product >> 64);
[](undefined) 4502    return r128;
[](undefined) 4503
[](undefined) 4504    /*
[](undefined) 4505     * MSVC for x64's _umul128 method.
[](undefined) 4506     *
[](undefined) 4507     * xxh_u64 _umul128(xxh_u64 Multiplier, xxh_u64 Multiplicand, xxh_u64 *HighProduct);
[](undefined) 4508     *
[](undefined) 4509     * This compiles to single operand MUL on x64.
[](undefined) 4510     */
[](undefined) 4511#elif (defined(_M_X64) || defined(_M_IA64)) && !defined(_M_ARM64EC)
[](undefined) 4512
[](undefined) 4513#ifndef _MSC_VER
[](undefined) 4514#   pragma intrinsic(_umul128)
[](undefined) 4515#endif
[](undefined) 4516    xxh_u64 product_high;
[](undefined) 4517    xxh_u64 const product_low = _umul128(lhs, rhs, &product_high);
[](undefined) 4518    [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) r128;
[](undefined) 4519    r128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = product_low;
[](undefined) 4520    r128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = product_high;
[](undefined) 4521    return r128;
[](undefined) 4522
[](undefined) 4523    /*
[](undefined) 4524     * MSVC for ARM64's __umulh method.
[](undefined) 4525     *
[](undefined) 4526     * This compiles to the same MUL + UMULH as GCC/Clang's __uint128_t method.
[](undefined) 4527     */
[](undefined) 4528#elif defined(_M_ARM64) || defined(_M_ARM64EC)
[](undefined) 4529
[](undefined) 4530#ifndef _MSC_VER
[](undefined) 4531#   pragma intrinsic(__umulh)
[](undefined) 4532#endif
[](undefined) 4533    [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) r128;
[](undefined) 4534    r128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = lhs * rhs;
[](undefined) 4535    r128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = __umulh(lhs, rhs);
[](undefined) 4536    return r128;
[](undefined) 4537
[](undefined) 4538#else
[](undefined) 4539    /*
[](undefined) 4540     * Portable scalar method. Optimized for 32-bit and 64-bit ALUs.
[](undefined) 4541     *
[](undefined) 4542     * This is a fast and simple grade school multiply, which is shown below
[](undefined) 4543     * with base 10 arithmetic instead of base 0x100000000.
[](undefined) 4544     *
[](undefined) 4545     *           9 3 // D2 lhs = 93
[](undefined) 4546     *         x 7 5 // D2 rhs = 75
[](undefined) 4547     *     ----------
[](undefined) 4548     *           1 5 // D2 lo_lo = (93 % 10) * (75 % 10) = 15
[](undefined) 4549     *         4 5 | // D2 hi_lo = (93 / 10) * (75 % 10) = 45
[](undefined) 4550     *         2 1 | // D2 lo_hi = (93 % 10) * (75 / 10) = 21
[](undefined) 4551     *     + 6 3 | | // D2 hi_hi = (93 / 10) * (75 / 10) = 63
[](undefined) 4552     *     ---------
[](undefined) 4553     *         2 7 | // D2 cross = (15 / 10) + (45 % 10) + 21 = 27
[](undefined) 4554     *     + 6 7 | | // D2 upper = (27 / 10) + (45 / 10) + 63 = 67
[](undefined) 4555     *     ---------
[](undefined) 4556     *       6 9 7 5 // D4 res = (27 * 10) + (15 % 10) + (67 * 100) = 6975
[](undefined) 4557     *
[](undefined) 4558     * The reasons for adding the products like this are:
[](undefined) 4559     *  1. It avoids manual carry tracking. Just like how
[](undefined) 4560     *     (9 * 9) + 9 + 9 = 99, the same applies with this for UINT64_MAX.
[](undefined) 4561     *     This avoids a lot of complexity.
[](undefined) 4562     *
[](undefined) 4563     *  2. It hints for, and on Clang, compiles to, the powerful UMAAL
[](undefined) 4564     *     instruction available in ARM's Digital Signal Processing extension
[](undefined) 4565     *     in 32-bit ARMv6 and later, which is shown below:
[](undefined) 4566     *
[](undefined) 4567     *         void UMAAL(xxh_u32 *RdLo, xxh_u32 *RdHi, xxh_u32 Rn, xxh_u32 Rm)
[](undefined) 4568     *         {
[](undefined) 4569     *             xxh_u64 product = (xxh_u64)*RdLo * (xxh_u64)*RdHi + Rn + Rm;
[](undefined) 4570     *             *RdLo = (xxh_u32)(product & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4571     *             *RdHi = (xxh_u32)(product >> 32);
[](undefined) 4572     *         }
[](undefined) 4573     *
[](undefined) 4574     *     This instruction was designed for efficient long multiplication, and
[](undefined) 4575     *     allows this to be calculated in only 4 instructions at speeds
[](undefined) 4576     *     comparable to some 64-bit ALUs.
[](undefined) 4577     *
[](undefined) 4578     *  3. It isn't terrible on other platforms. Usually this will be a couple
[](undefined) 4579     *     of 32-bit ADD/ADCs.
[](undefined) 4580     */
[](undefined) 4581
[](undefined) 4582    /* First calculate all of the cross products. */
[](undefined) 4583    xxh_u64 const lo_lo = XXH_mult32to64(lhs & 0xFFFFFFFF, rhs & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4584    xxh_u64 const hi_lo = XXH_mult32to64(lhs >> 32,        rhs & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4585    xxh_u64 const lo_hi = XXH_mult32to64(lhs & 0xFFFFFFFF, rhs >> 32);
[](undefined) 4586    xxh_u64 const hi_hi = XXH_mult32to64(lhs >> 32,        rhs >> 32);
[](undefined) 4587
[](undefined) 4588    /* Now add the products together. These will never overflow. */
[](undefined) 4589    xxh_u64 const cross = (lo_lo >> 32) + (hi_lo & 0xFFFFFFFF) + lo_hi;
[](undefined) 4590    xxh_u64 const upper = (hi_lo >> 32) + (cross >> 32)        + hi_hi;
[](undefined) 4591    xxh_u64 const lower = (cross << 32) | (lo_lo & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4592
[](undefined) 4593    [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) r128;
[](undefined) 4594    r128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = lower;
[](undefined) 4595    r128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = upper;
[](undefined) 4596    return r128;
[](undefined) 4597#endif
[](undefined) 4598}
[](undefined) 4599
[](undefined) 4610static xxh_u64
[](undefined) 4611XXH3_mul128_fold64(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs)
[](undefined) 4612{
[](undefined) 4613    [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) product = XXH_mult64to128(lhs, rhs);
[](undefined) 4614    return product.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef) ^ product.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8);
[](undefined) 4615}
[](undefined) 4616
[](undefined) 4618XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_CONSTF xxh_u64 XXH_xorshift64(xxh_u64 v64, int shift)
[](undefined) 4619{
[](undefined) 4620    XXH_ASSERT(0 <= shift && shift < 64);
[](undefined) 4621    return v64 ^ (v64 >> shift);
[](undefined) 4622}
[](undefined) 4623
[](undefined) 4624/*
[](undefined) 4625 * This is a fast avalanche stage,
[](undefined) 4626 * suitable when input bits are already partially mixed
[](undefined) 4627 */
[](undefined) 4628static [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) XXH3_avalanche(xxh_u64 h64)
[](undefined) 4629{
[](undefined) 4630    h64 = XXH_xorshift64(h64, 37);
[](undefined) 4631    h64 *= PRIME_MX1;
[](undefined) 4632    h64 = XXH_xorshift64(h64, 32);
[](undefined) 4633    return h64;
[](undefined) 4634}
[](undefined) 4635
[](undefined) 4636/*
[](undefined) 4637 * This is a stronger avalanche,
[](undefined) 4638 * inspired by Pelle Evensen's rrmxmx
[](undefined) 4639 * preferable when input has not been previously mixed
[](undefined) 4640 */
[](undefined) 4641static [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) XXH3_rrmxmx(xxh_u64 h64, xxh_u64 len)
[](undefined) 4642{
[](undefined) 4643    /* this mix is inspired by Pelle Evensen's rrmxmx */
[](undefined) 4644    h64 ^= XXH_rotl64(h64, 49) ^ XXH_rotl64(h64, 24);
[](undefined) 4645    h64 *= PRIME_MX2;
[](undefined) 4646    h64 ^= (h64 >> 35) + len ;
[](undefined) 4647    h64 *= PRIME_MX2;
[](undefined) 4648    return XXH_xorshift64(h64, 28);
[](undefined) 4649}
[](undefined) 4650
[](undefined) 4651
[](undefined) 4652/* ==========================================
[](undefined) 4653 * Short keys
[](undefined) 4654 * ==========================================
[](undefined) 4655 * One of the shortcomings of XXH32 and XXH64 was that their performance was
[](undefined) 4656 * sub-optimal on short lengths. It used an iterative algorithm which strongly
[](undefined) 4657 * favored lengths that were a multiple of 4 or 8.
[](undefined) 4658 *
[](undefined) 4659 * Instead of iterating over individual inputs, we use a set of single shot
[](undefined) 4660 * functions which piece together a range of lengths and operate in constant time.
[](undefined) 4661 *
[](undefined) 4662 * Additionally, the number of multiplies has been significantly reduced. This
[](undefined) 4663 * reduces latency, especially when emulating 64-bit multiplies on 32-bit.
[](undefined) 4664 *
[](undefined) 4665 * Depending on the platform, this may or may not be faster than XXH32, but it
[](undefined) 4666 * is almost guaranteed to be faster than XXH64.
[](undefined) 4667 */
[](undefined) 4668
[](undefined) 4669/*
[](undefined) 4670 * At very short lengths, there isn't enough input to fully hide secrets, or use
[](undefined) 4671 * the entire secret.
[](undefined) 4672 *
[](undefined) 4673 * There is also only a limited amount of mixing we can do before significantly
[](undefined) 4674 * impacting performance.
[](undefined) 4675 *
[](undefined) 4676 * Therefore, we use different sections of the secret and always mix two secret
[](undefined) 4677 * samples with an XOR. This should have no effect on performance on the
[](undefined) 4678 * seedless or withSeed variants because everything _should_ be constant folded
[](undefined) 4679 * by modern compilers.
[](undefined) 4680 *
[](undefined) 4681 * The XOR mixing hides individual parts of the secret and increases entropy.
[](undefined) 4682 *
[](undefined) 4683 * This adds an extra layer of strength for custom secrets.
[](undefined) 4684 */
[](undefined) 4685XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 4686XXH3_len_1to3_64b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 4687{
[](undefined) 4688    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 4689    XXH_ASSERT(1 <= len && len <= 3);
[](undefined) 4690    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 4691    /*
[](undefined) 4692     * len = 1: combined = { input[0], 0x01, input[0], input[0] }
[](undefined) 4693     * len = 2: combined = { input[1], 0x02, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 4694     * len = 3: combined = { input[2], 0x03, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 4695     */
[](undefined) 4696    {   xxh_u8  const c1 = input[0];
[](undefined) 4697        xxh_u8  const c2 = input[len >> 1];
[](undefined) 4698        xxh_u8  const c3 = input[len - 1];
[](undefined) 4699        xxh_u32 const combined = ((xxh_u32)c1 << 16) | ((xxh_u32)c2  << 24)
[](undefined) 4700                               | ((xxh_u32)c3 <<  0) | ((xxh_u32)len << 8);
[](undefined) 4701        xxh_u64 const bitflip = (XXH_readLE32(secret) ^ XXH_readLE32(secret+4)) + seed;
[](undefined) 4702        xxh_u64 const keyed = (xxh_u64)combined ^ bitflip;
[](undefined) 4703        return XXH64_avalanche(keyed);
[](undefined) 4704    }
[](undefined) 4705}

[](undefined) 4750
[](undefined) 4751/*
[](undefined) 4752 * DISCLAIMER: the mid-size code path built on XXH3_mix16B() (lengths 17 to 240)
[](undefined) 4753 * has known collision weaknesses. XXH3 is a non-cryptographic hash: collision
[](undefined) 4754 * resistance is not one of its guarantees, and the seed (or custom secret) only
[](undefined) 4755 * makes intentional collisions *harder*, it is not a security boundary.
[](undefined) 4756 * These weaknesses are nonetheless documented here, so that users relying on a
[](undefined) 4757 * seed for a low per-pair collision probability know what to expect.
[](undefined) 4758 *
[](undefined) 4759 * 1) XXH3_mul128_fold64() does not separate complemented multiplicands.
[](undefined) 4760 *    Modulo 2^128, and with s = a + b + 1 :
[](undefined) 4761 *        (~a) * (~b) == a*b + s - (s + 1) * 2^64
[](undefined) 4762 *    so the low half of the product gains `s` while the high half loses `s`.
[](undefined) 4763 *    Since carry chains are short, both halves happen to flip the same bits
[](undefined) 4764 *    fairly often, leaving their xor (the "fold") unchanged with probability
[](undefined) 4765 *    ~2^-27, instead of the ideal 2^-64. Complementing a single multiplicand
[](undefined) 4766 *    behaves the same way.
[](undefined) 4767 *
[](undefined) 4768 *    Consequence for XXH3_64bits(): for any length in [32, 240], two messages
[](undefined) 4769 *    differing only by the complement of their first 8 (or first 16) bytes
[](undefined) 4770 *    collide with probability ~2^-27 over a uniformly random secret. The
[](undefined) 4771 *    _withSeed variant is also affected, but its collision probability depends
[](undefined) 4772 *    on the chosen pair because the 64-bit seed produces a structured secret.
[](undefined) 4773 *    A fixed pair has been measured to collide with probability ~2^-21 over
[](undefined) 4774 *    random seeds. This is an observed result, not a known upper bound.
[](undefined) 4775 *
[](undefined) 4776 *    Consequence for XXH128(): XXH128_mix32B() mixes each 16-byte chunk a
[](undefined) 4777 *    second time as a raw 64-bit sum, which normally breaks the pattern above.
[](undefined) 4778 *    But that sum is *also* invariant under complementation when
[](undefined) 4779 *    w0 + w1 == 2^64 - 1, and under that additional constraint both output
[](undefined) 4780 *    halves collide simultaneously, again with probability ~2^-27 over a
[](undefined) 4781 *    uniformly random secret. The seeded variant is also affected, and its
[](undefined) 4782 *    exact probability can similarly depend on the chosen pair.
[](undefined) 4783 *    So the 128-bit variant is _not_ immune, contrarily to what was previously
[](undefined) 4784 *    believed, and its effective margin here (2^-27 vs. an ideal 2^-128) is
[](undefined) 4785 *    even thinner than the 64-bit one.
[](undefined) 4786 *
[](undefined) 4787 *    Detailed analysis and measurements in
[](undefined) 4788 *    https://github.com/Cyan4973/xxHash/issues/1127 .
[](undefined) 4789 *
[](undefined) 4790 * 2) *seed-dependent* multicollisions, due to multiplication by zero: when one
[](undefined) 4791 *    of the two multiplicands below is zero, the whole 16-byte chunk stops
[](undefined) 4792 *    contributing to the hash, so all chunks sharing this property collide.
[](undefined) 4793 *    For random inputs, this is a 1 in 2^63 chance of 8 consecutive bytes
[](undefined) 4794 *    cancelling out, in a function invoked at most 16 times per hash, hence
[](undefined) 4795 *    essentially unreachable by accident. But it is trivially reachable by an
[](undefined) 4796 *    attacker who knows the secret (i.e. the unseeded variants). For
[](undefined) 4797 *    comparison, classic UMAC takes a 1 in 2^31 chance of 4 consecutive bytes
[](undefined) 4798 *    cancelling out the secret an arbitrary number of times (addressed for
[](undefined) 4799 *    long inputs in XXH3_accumulate_512).
[](undefined) 4800 *
[](undefined) 4801 * None of the above can be fixed without changing XXH3_mix16B(), which would
[](undefined) 4802 * change every hash value in the [17, 240] range, and XXH3 output values are
[](undefined) 4803 * now frozen. Therefore, if adversarial collision resistance matters for your
[](undefined) 4804 * use case, use a cryptographic hash or a MAC instead.
[](undefined) 4805 */
[](undefined) 4806XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64 XXH3_mix16B(const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 4807                                     const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT secret, xxh_u64 seed64)
[](undefined) 4808{
[](undefined) 4809#if defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__) /* GCC, not Clang */ \
[](undefined) 4810  && defined(__i386__) && defined(__SSE2__)  /* x86 + SSE2 */ \
[](undefined) 4811  && !defined(XXH_ENABLE_AUTOVECTORIZE)      /* Define to disable like XXH32 hack */
[](undefined) 4812    /*
[](undefined) 4813     * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 4814     * GCC for x86 tends to autovectorize the 128-bit multiply, resulting in
[](undefined) 4815     * slower code.
[](undefined) 4816     *
[](undefined) 4817     * By forcing seed64 into a register, we disrupt the cost model and
[](undefined) 4818     * cause it to scalarize. See `XXH32_round()`
[](undefined) 4819     *
[](undefined) 4820     * FIXME: Clang's output is still _much_ faster -- On an AMD Ryzen 3600,
[](undefined) 4821     * XXH3_64bits @ len=240 runs at 4.6 GB/s with Clang 9, but 3.3 GB/s on
[](undefined) 4822     * GCC 9.2, despite both emitting scalar code.
[](undefined) 4823     *
[](undefined) 4824     * GCC generates much better scalar code than Clang for the rest of XXH3,
[](undefined) 4825     * which is why finding a more optimal codepath is an interest.
[](undefined) 4826     */
[](undefined) 4827    XXH_COMPILER_GUARD(seed64);
[](undefined) 4828#endif
[](undefined) 4829    {   xxh_u64 const input_lo = XXH_readLE64(input);
[](undefined) 4830        xxh_u64 const input_hi = XXH_readLE64(input+8);
[](undefined) 4831        return XXH3_mul128_fold64(
[](undefined) 4832            input_lo ^ (XXH_readLE64(secret)   + seed64),
[](undefined) 4833            input_hi ^ (XXH_readLE64(secret+8) - seed64)
[](undefined) 4834        );
[](undefined) 4835    }
[](undefined) 4836}

[](undefined) 4905         *

[](undefined) 4908         *

[](undefined) 4935/* =======     Long Keys     ======= */
[](undefined) 4936
[](undefined) 4937#define XXH_STRIPE_LEN 64
[](undefined) 4938#define XXH_SECRET_CONSUME_RATE 8   /* nb of secret bytes consumed at each accumulation */
[](undefined) 4939#define XXH_ACC_NB (XXH_STRIPE_LEN / sizeof(xxh_u64))
[](undefined) 4940
[](undefined) 4941#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 4942#  define STRIPE_LEN XXH_STRIPE_LEN
[](undefined) 4943#  define ACC_NB XXH_ACC_NB
[](undefined) 4944#endif
[](undefined) 4945
[](undefined) 4946#ifndef XXH_PREFETCH_DIST
[](undefined) 4947#  ifdef __clang__
[](undefined) 4948#    define XXH_PREFETCH_DIST 320
[](undefined) 4949#  else
[](undefined) 4950#    if (XXH_VECTOR == XXH_AVX512)
[](undefined) 4951#      define XXH_PREFETCH_DIST 512
[](undefined) 4952#    else
[](undefined) 4953#      define XXH_PREFETCH_DIST 384
[](undefined) 4954#    endif
[](undefined) 4955#  endif  /* __clang__ */
[](undefined) 4956#endif  /* XXH_PREFETCH_DIST */
[](undefined) 4957
[](undefined) 4958/*
[](undefined) 4959 * These macros are to generate an XXH3_accumulate() function.
[](undefined) 4960 * The two arguments select the name suffix and target attribute.
[](undefined) 4961 *
[](undefined) 4962 * The name of this symbol is XXH3_accumulate_<name>() and it calls
[](undefined) 4963 * XXH3_accumulate_512_<name>().
[](undefined) 4964 *
[](undefined) 4965 * It may be useful to hand implement this function if the compiler fails to
[](undefined) 4966 * optimize the inline function.
[](undefined) 4967 */
[](undefined) 4968#define XXH3_ACCUMULATE_TEMPLATE(name)                      \
[](undefined) 4969void                                                        \
[](undefined) 4970XXH3_accumulate_##name(xxh_u64* XXH_RESTRICT acc,           \
[](undefined) 4971                       const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT input,    \
[](undefined) 4972                       const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT secret,   \
[](undefined) 4973                       size_t nbStripes)                    \
[](undefined) 4974{                                                           \
[](undefined) 4975    size_t n;                                               \
[](undefined) 4976    for (n = 0; n < nbStripes; n++ ) {                      \
[](undefined) 4977        const xxh_u8* const in = input + n*XXH_STRIPE_LEN;  \
[](undefined) 4978        XXH_PREFETCH(in + XXH_PREFETCH_DIST);               \
[](undefined) 4979        XXH3_accumulate_512_##name(                         \
[](undefined) 4980                 acc,                                       \
[](undefined) 4981                 in,                                        \
[](undefined) 4982                 secret + n*XXH_SECRET_CONSUME_RATE);       \
[](undefined) 4983    }                                                       \
[](undefined) 4984}

[](undefined) 4993/* Several intrinsic functions below are supposed to accept __int64 as argument,
[](undefined) 4994 * as documented in https://software.intel.com/sites/landingpage/IntrinsicsGuide/ .
[](undefined) 4995 * However, several environments do not define __int64 type,
[](undefined) 4996 * requiring a workaround.
[](undefined) 4997 */
[](undefined) 4998#if !defined (__VMS) \
[](undefined) 4999  && (defined (__cplusplus) \
[](undefined) 5000  || (defined (__STDC_VERSION__) && (__STDC_VERSION__ >= 199901L) /* C99 */) )
[](undefined) 5001    typedef int64_t xxh_i64;
[](undefined) 5002#else
[](undefined) 5003    /* the following type must have a width of 64-bit */
[](undefined) 5004    typedef long long xxh_i64;
[](undefined) 5005#endif
[](undefined) 5006
[](undefined) 5007
[](undefined) 5008/*
[](undefined) 5009 * XXH3_accumulate_512 is the tightest loop for long inputs, and it is the most optimized.
[](undefined) 5010 *
[](undefined) 5011 * It is a hardened version of UMAC, based off of FARSH's implementation.
[](undefined) 5012 *
[](undefined) 5013 * This was chosen because it adapts quite well to 32-bit, 64-bit, and SIMD
[](undefined) 5014 * implementations, and it is ridiculously fast.
[](undefined) 5015 *
[](undefined) 5016 * We harden it by mixing the original input to the accumulators as well as the product.
[](undefined) 5017 *
[](undefined) 5018 * This means that in the (relatively likely) case of a multiply by zero, the
[](undefined) 5019 * original input is preserved.
[](undefined) 5020 *
[](undefined) 5021 * On 128-bit inputs, we swap 64-bit pairs when we add the input to improve
[](undefined) 5022 * cross-pollination, as otherwise the upper and lower halves would be
[](undefined) 5023 * essentially independent.
[](undefined) 5024 *
[](undefined) 5025 * This doesn't matter on 64-bit hashes since they all get merged together in
[](undefined) 5026 * the end, so we skip the extra step.
[](undefined) 5027 *
[](undefined) 5028 * Both XXH3_64bits and XXH3_128bits use this subroutine.
[](undefined) 5029 */
[](undefined) 5030
[](undefined) 5031#if (XXH_VECTOR == XXH_AVX512) \
[](undefined) 5032     || (defined(XXH_DISPATCH_AVX512) && XXH_DISPATCH_AVX512 != 0)
[](undefined) 5033
[](undefined) 5034#ifndef XXH_TARGET_AVX512
[](undefined)[ 5035](group__dispatch.html#ga94d06a353acaa62bbd9fbf65e865a282)# define XXH_TARGET_AVX512  /* disable attribute target */
[](undefined) 5036#endif
[](undefined) 5037
[](undefined) 5038XXH_FORCE_INLINE [XXH_TARGET_AVX512](group__dispatch.html#ga94d06a353acaa62bbd9fbf65e865a282) void
[](undefined) 5039XXH3_accumulate_512_avx512(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 5040                     const void* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 5041                     const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5042{
[](undefined) 5043    __m512i* const xacc = (__m512i *) acc;
[](undefined) 5044    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 63) == 0);
[](undefined) 5045    XXH_STATIC_ASSERT(XXH_STRIPE_LEN == sizeof(__m512i));
[](undefined) 5046
[](undefined) 5047    {
[](undefined) 5048        /* data_vec    = input[0]; */
[](undefined) 5049        __m512i const data_vec    = _mm512_loadu_si512   (input);
[](undefined) 5050        /* key_vec     = secret[0]; */
[](undefined) 5051        __m512i const key_vec     = _mm512_loadu_si512   (secret);
[](undefined) 5052        /* data_key    = data_vec ^ key_vec; */
[](undefined) 5053        __m512i const data_key    = _mm512_xor_si512     (data_vec, key_vec);
[](undefined) 5054        /* data_key_lo = data_key >> 32; */
[](undefined) 5055        __m512i const data_key_lo = _mm512_srli_epi64 (data_key, 32);
[](undefined) 5056        /* product     = (data_key & 0xffffffff) * (data_key_lo & 0xffffffff); */
[](undefined) 5057        __m512i const product     = _mm512_mul_epu32     (data_key, data_key_lo);
[](undefined) 5058        /* xacc[0] += swap(data_vec); */
[](undefined) 5059        __m512i const data_swap = _mm512_shuffle_epi32(data_vec, (_MM_PERM_ENUM)_MM_SHUFFLE(1, 0, 3, 2));
[](undefined) 5060        __m512i const sum       = _mm512_add_epi64(*xacc, data_swap);
[](undefined) 5061        /* xacc[0] += product; */
[](undefined) 5062        *xacc = _mm512_add_epi64(product, sum);
[](undefined) 5063    }
[](undefined) 5064}
[](undefined) 5065XXH_FORCE_INLINE [XXH_TARGET_AVX512](group__dispatch.html#ga94d06a353acaa62bbd9fbf65e865a282) XXH3_ACCUMULATE_TEMPLATE(avx512)
[](undefined) 5066
[](undefined) 5067/*
[](undefined) 5068 * XXH3_scrambleAcc: Scrambles the accumulators to improve mixing.
[](undefined) 5069 *
[](undefined) 5070 * Multiplication isn't perfect, as explained by Google in HighwayHash:
[](undefined) 5071 *
[](undefined) 5072 *  // Multiplication mixes/scrambles bytes 0-7 of the 64-bit result to
[](undefined) 5073 *  // varying degrees. In descending order of goodness, bytes
[](undefined) 5074 *  // 3 4 2 5 1 6 0 7 have quality 228 224 164 160 100 96 36 32.
[](undefined) 5075 *  // As expected, the upper and lower bytes are much worse.
[](undefined) 5076 *
[](undefined) 5077 * Source: https://github.com/google/highwayhash/blob/0aaf66b/highwayhash/hh_avx2.h#L291
[](undefined) 5078 *
[](undefined) 5079 * Since our algorithm uses a pseudorandom secret to add some variance into the
[](undefined) 5080 * mix, we don't need to (or want to) mix as often or as much as HighwayHash does.
[](undefined) 5081 *
[](undefined) 5082 * This isn't as tight as XXH3_accumulate, but still written in SIMD to avoid
[](undefined) 5083 * extraction.
[](undefined) 5084 *
[](undefined) 5085 * Both XXH3_64bits and XXH3_128bits use this subroutine.
[](undefined) 5086 */
[](undefined) 5087
[](undefined) 5088XXH_FORCE_INLINE [XXH_TARGET_AVX512](group__dispatch.html#ga94d06a353acaa62bbd9fbf65e865a282) void
[](undefined) 5089XXH3_scrambleAcc_avx512(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc, const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5090{
[](undefined) 5091    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 63) == 0);
[](undefined) 5092    XXH_STATIC_ASSERT(XXH_STRIPE_LEN == sizeof(__m512i));
[](undefined) 5093    {   __m512i* const xacc = (__m512i*) acc;
[](undefined) 5094        const __m512i prime32 = _mm512_set1_epi32((int)[XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f));
[](undefined) 5095
[](undefined) 5096        /* xacc[0] ^= (xacc[0] >> 47) */
[](undefined) 5097        __m512i const acc_vec     = *xacc;
[](undefined) 5098        __m512i const shifted     = _mm512_srli_epi64    (acc_vec, 47);
[](undefined) 5099        /* xacc[0] ^= secret; */
[](undefined) 5100        __m512i const key_vec     = _mm512_loadu_si512   (secret);
[](undefined) 5101        __m512i const data_key    = _mm512_ternarylogic_epi32(key_vec, acc_vec, shifted, 0x96 /* key_vec ^ acc_vec ^ shifted */);
[](undefined) 5102
[](undefined) 5103        /* xacc[0] *= XXH_PRIME32_1; */
[](undefined) 5104        __m512i const data_key_hi = _mm512_srli_epi64 (data_key, 32);
[](undefined) 5105        __m512i const prod_lo     = _mm512_mul_epu32     (data_key, prime32);
[](undefined) 5106        __m512i const prod_hi     = _mm512_mul_epu32     (data_key_hi, prime32);
[](undefined) 5107        *xacc = _mm512_add_epi64(prod_lo, _mm512_slli_epi64(prod_hi, 32));
[](undefined) 5108    }
[](undefined) 5109}
[](undefined) 5110

[](undefined) 5363XXH3_scalarScrambleRound(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,

[](undefined) 5365
[](undefined) 5389
[](undefined) 5390XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5391XXH3_accumulate_512_neon( void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 5392                    const void* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 5393                    const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5394{
[](undefined) 5395    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 15) == 0);
[](undefined) 5396    XXH_STATIC_ASSERT([XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) > 0 && [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) <= XXH_ACC_NB && [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) % 2 == 0);
[](undefined) 5397    {   /* GCC for darwin arm64 does not like aliasing here */
[](undefined) 5398        xxh_aliasing_uint64x2_t* const xacc = (xxh_aliasing_uint64x2_t*) acc;
[](undefined) 5399        /* We don't use a uint32x4_t pointer because it causes bus errors on ARMv7. */
[](undefined) 5400        uint8_t const* xinput = (const uint8_t *) input;
[](undefined) 5401        uint8_t const* xsecret  = (const uint8_t *) secret;
[](undefined) 5402
[](undefined) 5403        size_t i;
[](undefined) 5404#ifdef __wasm_simd128__
[](undefined) 5405        /*
[](undefined) 5406         * On WASM SIMD128, Clang emits direct address loads when XXH3_kSecret
[](undefined) 5407         * is constant propagated, which results in it converting it to this
[](undefined) 5408         * inside the loop:
[](undefined) 5409         *
[](undefined) 5410         *    a = v128.load(XXH3_kSecret +  0 + $secret_offset, offset = 0)
[](undefined) 5411         *    b = v128.load(XXH3_kSecret + 16 + $secret_offset, offset = 0)
[](undefined) 5412         *    ...
[](undefined) 5413         *
[](undefined) 5414         * This requires a full 32-bit address immediate (and therefore a 6 byte
[](undefined) 5415         * instruction) as well as an add for each offset.
[](undefined) 5416         *
[](undefined) 5417         * Putting an asm guard prevents it from folding (at the cost of losing
[](undefined) 5418         * the alignment hint), and uses the free offset in `v128.load` instead
[](undefined) 5419         * of adding secret_offset each time which overall reduces code size by
[](undefined) 5420         * about a kilobyte and improves performance.
[](undefined) 5421         */
[](undefined) 5422        XXH_COMPILER_GUARD(xsecret);
[](undefined) 5423#endif
[](undefined) 5424        /* Scalar lanes use the normal scalarRound routine */
[](undefined) 5425        for (i = [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c); i < XXH_ACC_NB; i++) {
[](undefined) 5426            XXH3_scalarRound(acc, input, secret, i);
[](undefined) 5427        }
[](undefined) 5428        i = 0;
[](undefined) 5429        /* 4 NEON lanes at a time. */
[](undefined) 5430        for (; i+1 < [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) / 2; i+=2) {
[](undefined) 5431            /* data_vec = xinput[i]; */
[](undefined) 5432            uint64x2_t data_vec_1 = XXH_vld1q_u64(xinput  + (i * 16));
[](undefined) 5433            uint64x2_t data_vec_2 = XXH_vld1q_u64(xinput  + ((i+1) * 16));
[](undefined) 5434            /* key_vec  = xsecret[i];  */
[](undefined) 5435            uint64x2_t key_vec_1  = XXH_vld1q_u64(xsecret + (i * 16));
[](undefined) 5436            uint64x2_t key_vec_2  = XXH_vld1q_u64(xsecret + ((i+1) * 16));
[](undefined) 5437            /* data_swap = swap(data_vec) */
[](undefined) 5438            uint64x2_t data_swap_1 = vextq_u64(data_vec_1, data_vec_1, 1);
[](undefined) 5439            uint64x2_t data_swap_2 = vextq_u64(data_vec_2, data_vec_2, 1);
[](undefined) 5440            /* data_key = data_vec ^ key_vec; */
[](undefined) 5441            uint64x2_t data_key_1 = veorq_u64(data_vec_1, key_vec_1);
[](undefined) 5442            uint64x2_t data_key_2 = veorq_u64(data_vec_2, key_vec_2);
[](undefined) 5443
[](undefined) 5444            /*
[](undefined) 5445             * If we reinterpret the 64x2 vectors as 32x4 vectors, we can use a
[](undefined) 5446             * de-interleave operation for 4 lanes in 1 step with `vuzpq_u32` to
[](undefined) 5447             * get one vector with the low 32 bits of each lane, and one vector
[](undefined) 5448             * with the high 32 bits of each lane.
[](undefined) 5449             *
[](undefined) 5450             * The intrinsic returns a double vector because the original ARMv7-a
[](undefined) 5451             * instruction modified both arguments in place. AArch64 and SIMD128 emit
[](undefined) 5452             * two instructions from this intrinsic.
[](undefined) 5453             *
[](undefined) 5454             *  [ dk11L | dk11H | dk12L | dk12H ] -> [ dk11L | dk12L | dk21L | dk22L ]
[](undefined) 5455             *  [ dk21L | dk21H | dk22L | dk22H ] -> [ dk11H | dk12H | dk21H | dk22H ]
[](undefined) 5456             */
[](undefined) 5457            uint32x4x2_t unzipped = vuzpq_u32(
[](undefined) 5458                vreinterpretq_u32_u64(data_key_1),
[](undefined) 5459                vreinterpretq_u32_u64(data_key_2)
[](undefined) 5460            );
[](undefined) 5461            /* data_key_lo = data_key & 0xFFFFFFFF */
[](undefined) 5462            uint32x4_t data_key_lo = unzipped.val[0];
[](undefined) 5463            /* data_key_hi = data_key >> 32 */
[](undefined) 5464            uint32x4_t data_key_hi = unzipped.val[1];
[](undefined) 5465            /*
[](undefined) 5466             * Then, we can split the vectors horizontally and multiply which, as for most
[](undefined) 5467             * widening intrinsics, have a variant that works on both high half vectors
[](undefined) 5468             * for free on AArch64. A similar instruction is available on SIMD128.
[](undefined) 5469             *
[](undefined) 5470             * sum = data_swap + (u64x2) data_key_lo * (u64x2) data_key_hi
[](undefined) 5471             */
[](undefined) 5472            uint64x2_t sum_1 = XXH_vmlal_low_u32(data_swap_1, data_key_lo, data_key_hi);
[](undefined) 5473            uint64x2_t sum_2 = XXH_vmlal_high_u32(data_swap_2, data_key_lo, data_key_hi);
[](undefined) 5474            /*
[](undefined) 5475             * Clang reorders
[](undefined) 5476             *    a += b * c;     // umlal   swap.2d, dkl.2s, dkh.2s
[](undefined) 5477             *    c += a;         // add     acc.2d, acc.2d, swap.2d
[](undefined) 5478             * to
[](undefined) 5479             *    c += a;         // add     acc.2d, acc.2d, swap.2d
[](undefined) 5480             *    c += b * c;     // umlal   acc.2d, dkl.2s, dkh.2s
[](undefined) 5481             *
[](undefined) 5482             * While it would make sense in theory since the addition is faster,
[](undefined) 5483             * for reasons likely related to umlal being limited to certain NEON
[](undefined) 5484             * pipelines, this is worse. A compiler guard fixes this.
[](undefined) 5485             */
[](undefined) 5486            XXH_COMPILER_GUARD_CLANG_NEON(sum_1);
[](undefined) 5487            XXH_COMPILER_GUARD_CLANG_NEON(sum_2);
[](undefined) 5488            /* xacc[i] = acc_vec + sum; */
[](undefined) 5489            xacc[i]   = vaddq_u64(xacc[i], sum_1);
[](undefined) 5490            xacc[i+1] = vaddq_u64(xacc[i+1], sum_2);
[](undefined) 5491        }
[](undefined) 5492        /* Operate on the remaining NEON lanes 2 at a time. */
[](undefined) 5493        for (; i < [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) / 2; i++) {
[](undefined) 5494            /* data_vec = xinput[i]; */
[](undefined) 5495            uint64x2_t data_vec = XXH_vld1q_u64(xinput  + (i * 16));
[](undefined) 5496            /* key_vec  = xsecret[i];  */
[](undefined) 5497            uint64x2_t key_vec  = XXH_vld1q_u64(xsecret + (i * 16));
[](undefined) 5498            /* acc_vec_2 = swap(data_vec) */
[](undefined) 5499            uint64x2_t data_swap = vextq_u64(data_vec, data_vec, 1);
[](undefined) 5500            /* data_key = data_vec ^ key_vec; */
[](undefined) 5501            uint64x2_t data_key = veorq_u64(data_vec, key_vec);
[](undefined) 5502            /* For two lanes, just use VMOVN and VSHRN. */
[](undefined) 5503            /* data_key_lo = data_key & 0xFFFFFFFF; */
[](undefined) 5504            uint32x2_t data_key_lo = vmovn_u64(data_key);
[](undefined) 5505            /* data_key_hi = data_key >> 32; */
[](undefined) 5506            uint32x2_t data_key_hi = vshrn_n_u64(data_key, 32);
[](undefined) 5507            /* sum = data_swap + (u64x2) data_key_lo * (u64x2) data_key_hi; */
[](undefined) 5508            uint64x2_t sum = vmlal_u32(data_swap, data_key_lo, data_key_hi);
[](undefined) 5509            /* Same Clang workaround as before */
[](undefined) 5510            XXH_COMPILER_GUARD_CLANG_NEON(sum);
[](undefined) 5511            /* xacc[i] = acc_vec + sum; */
[](undefined) 5512            xacc[i] = vaddq_u64 (xacc[i], sum);
[](undefined) 5513        }
[](undefined) 5514    }
[](undefined) 5515}
[](undefined) 5516XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH3_ACCUMULATE_TEMPLATE(neon)

[](undefined) 5518XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5519XXH3_scrambleAcc_neon(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc, const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5520{
[](undefined) 5521    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 15) == 0);
[](undefined) 5522
[](undefined) 5523    {   xxh_aliasing_uint64x2_t* xacc       = (xxh_aliasing_uint64x2_t*) acc;
[](undefined) 5524        uint8_t const* xsecret = (uint8_t const*) secret;
[](undefined) 5525
[](undefined) 5526        size_t i;
[](undefined) 5527        /* WASM uses operator overloads and doesn't need these. */
[](undefined) 5528#ifndef __wasm_simd128__
[](undefined) 5529        /* { prime32_1, prime32_1 } */
[](undefined) 5530        uint32x2_t const kPrimeLo = vdup_n_u32([XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f));
[](undefined) 5531        /* { 0, prime32_1, 0, prime32_1 } */
[](undefined) 5532        uint32x4_t const kPrimeHi = vreinterpretq_u32_u64(vdupq_n_u64((xxh_u64)[XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f) << 32));
[](undefined) 5533#endif
[](undefined) 5534
[](undefined) 5535        /* AArch64 uses both scalar and neon at the same time */
[](undefined) 5536        for (i = [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c); i < XXH_ACC_NB; i++) {
[](undefined) 5537            XXH3_scalarScrambleRound(acc, secret, i);
[](undefined) 5538        }
[](undefined) 5539        for (i=0; i < [XXH3_NEON_LANES](group__tuning.html#gac799ce966f18e4837f7fdc3d51917e9c) / 2; i++) {
[](undefined) 5540            /* xacc[i] ^= (xacc[i] >> 47); */
[](undefined) 5541            uint64x2_t acc_vec  = xacc[i];
[](undefined) 5542            uint64x2_t shifted  = vshrq_n_u64(acc_vec, 47);
[](undefined) 5543            uint64x2_t data_vec = veorq_u64(acc_vec, shifted);
[](undefined) 5544
[](undefined) 5545            /* xacc[i] ^= xsecret[i]; */
[](undefined) 5546            uint64x2_t key_vec  = XXH_vld1q_u64(xsecret + (i * 16));
[](undefined) 5547            uint64x2_t data_key = veorq_u64(data_vec, key_vec);
[](undefined) 5548            /* xacc[i] *= XXH_PRIME32_1 */
[](undefined) 5549#ifdef __wasm_simd128__
[](undefined) 5550            /* SIMD128 has multiply by u64x2, use it instead of expanding and scalarizing */
[](undefined) 5551            xacc[i] = data_key * [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f);
[](undefined) 5552#else
[](undefined) 5553            /*
[](undefined) 5554             * Expanded version with portable NEON intrinsics
[](undefined) 5555             *
[](undefined) 5556             *    lo(x) * lo(y) + (hi(x) * lo(y) << 32)
[](undefined) 5557             *
[](undefined) 5558             * prod_hi = hi(data_key) * lo(prime) << 32
[](undefined) 5559             *
[](undefined) 5560             * Since we only need 32 bits of this multiply a trick can be used, reinterpreting the vector
[](undefined) 5561             * as a uint32x4_t and multiplying by { 0, prime, 0, prime } to cancel out the unwanted bits
[](undefined) 5562             * and avoid the shift.
[](undefined) 5563             */
[](undefined) 5564            uint32x4_t prod_hi = vmulq_u32 (vreinterpretq_u32_u64(data_key), kPrimeHi);
[](undefined) 5565            /* Extract low bits for vmlal_u32  */
[](undefined) 5566            uint32x2_t data_key_lo = vmovn_u64(data_key);
[](undefined) 5567            /* xacc[i] = prod_hi + lo(data_key) * XXH_PRIME32_1; */
[](undefined) 5568            xacc[i] = vmlal_u32(vreinterpretq_u64_u32(prod_hi), data_key_lo, kPrimeLo);
[](undefined) 5569#endif
[](undefined) 5570        }
[](undefined) 5571    }
[](undefined) 5572}
[](undefined) 5573#endif

[](undefined) 5580                    const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5581{

[](undefined) 5583    xxh_aliasing_u64x2* const xacc = (xxh_aliasing_u64x2*) acc;

[](undefined) 5585    xxh_u8 const* const xsecret  = (xxh_u8 const*) secret;    /* no alignment restriction */
[](undefined) 5586    xxh_u64x2 const v32 = { 32, 32 };
[](undefined) 5587    size_t i;
[](undefined) 5588    for (i = 0; i < XXH_STRIPE_LEN / sizeof(xxh_u64x2); i++) {
[](undefined) 5589        /* data_vec = xinput[i]; */
[](undefined) 5590        xxh_u64x2 const data_vec = XXH_vec_loadu(xinput + 16*i);
[](undefined) 5591        /* key_vec = xsecret[i]; */
[](undefined) 5592        xxh_u64x2 const key_vec  = XXH_vec_loadu(xsecret + 16*i);
[](undefined) 5593        xxh_u64x2 const data_key = data_vec ^ key_vec;
[](undefined) 5594        /* shuffled = (data_key << 32) | (data_key >> 32); */

[](undefined) 5601
[](undefined) 5602        /* swap high and low halves */

[](undefined) 5605#else
[](undefined) 5606        acc_vec += vec_xxpermdi(data_vec, data_vec, 2);
[](undefined) 5607#endif
[](undefined) 5608        xacc[i] = acc_vec;
[](undefined) 5609    }

[](undefined) 5613XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5614XXH3_scrambleAcc_vsx(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc, const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5615{
[](undefined) 5616    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 15) == 0);
[](undefined) 5617
[](undefined) 5618    {   xxh_aliasing_u64x2* const xacc = (xxh_aliasing_u64x2*) acc;
[](undefined) 5619        const xxh_u8* const xsecret = (const xxh_u8*) secret;
[](undefined) 5620        /* constants */
[](undefined) 5621        xxh_u64x2 const v32  = { 32, 32 };
[](undefined) 5622        xxh_u64x2 const v47 = { 47, 47 };
[](undefined) 5623        xxh_u32x4 const prime = { [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f), [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f), [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f), [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f) };
[](undefined) 5624        size_t i;
[](undefined) 5625        for (i = 0; i < XXH_STRIPE_LEN / sizeof(xxh_u64x2); i++) {
[](undefined) 5626            /* xacc[i] ^= (xacc[i] >> 47); */
[](undefined) 5627            xxh_u64x2 const acc_vec  = xacc[i];
[](undefined) 5628            xxh_u64x2 const data_vec = acc_vec ^ (acc_vec >> v47);
[](undefined) 5629
[](undefined) 5630            /* xacc[i] ^= xsecret[i]; */
[](undefined) 5631            xxh_u64x2 const key_vec  = XXH_vec_loadu(xsecret + 16*i);
[](undefined) 5632            xxh_u64x2 const data_key = data_vec ^ key_vec;
[](undefined) 5633
[](undefined) 5634            /* xacc[i] *= XXH_PRIME32_1 */
[](undefined) 5635            /* prod_lo = ((xxh_u64x2)data_key & 0xFFFFFFFF) * ((xxh_u64x2)prime & 0xFFFFFFFF);  */
[](undefined) 5636            xxh_u64x2 const prod_even  = XXH_vec_mule((xxh_u32x4)data_key, prime);
[](undefined) 5637            /* prod_hi = ((xxh_u64x2)data_key >> 32) * ((xxh_u64x2)prime >> 32);  */
[](undefined) 5638            xxh_u64x2 const prod_odd  = XXH_vec_mulo((xxh_u32x4)data_key, prime);
[](undefined) 5639            xacc[i] = prod_odd + (prod_even << v32);
[](undefined) 5640    }   }

[](undefined) 5647XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5648XXH3_accumulate_512_sve( void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,

[](undefined) 5650                   const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5651{
[](undefined) 5652    uint64_t *xacc = (uint64_t *)acc;
[](undefined) 5653    const uint64_t *xinput = (const uint64_t *)(const void *)input;
[](undefined) 5654    const uint64_t *xsecret = (const uint64_t *)(const void *)secret;
[](undefined) 5655    svuint64_t kSwap = sveor_n_u64_z(svptrue_b64(), svindex_u64(0, 1), 1);
[](undefined) 5656    uint64_t element_count = svcntd();

[](undefined) 5658        svbool_t mask = svptrue_pat_b64(SV_VL8);
[](undefined) 5659        svuint64_t vacc = svld1_u64(mask, xacc);
[](undefined) 5660        ACCRND(vacc, 0);
[](undefined) 5661        svst1_u64(mask, xacc, vacc);
[](undefined) 5662    } else if (element_count == 2) {   /* sve128 */

[](undefined) 5664        svuint64_t acc0 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 0);
[](undefined) 5665        svuint64_t acc1 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 2);
[](undefined) 5666        svuint64_t acc2 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 4);
[](undefined) 5667        svuint64_t acc3 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 6);

[](undefined) 5673        svst1_u64(mask, xacc + 2, acc1);
[](undefined) 5674        svst1_u64(mask, xacc + 4, acc2);
[](undefined) 5675        svst1_u64(mask, xacc + 6, acc3);
[](undefined) 5676    } else {
[](undefined) 5677        svbool_t mask = svptrue_pat_b64(SV_VL4);
[](undefined) 5678        svuint64_t acc0 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 0);
[](undefined) 5679        svuint64_t acc1 = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 4);
[](undefined) 5680        ACCRND(acc0, 0);
[](undefined) 5681        ACCRND(acc1, 4);
[](undefined) 5682        svst1_u64(mask, xacc + 0, acc0);
[](undefined) 5683        svst1_u64(mask, xacc + 4, acc1);
[](undefined) 5684    }

[](undefined) 5686
[](undefined) 5687XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5688XXH3_accumulate_sve(xxh_u64* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 5689               const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 5690               const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT secret,
[](undefined) 5691               size_t nbStripes)
[](undefined) 5692{
[](undefined) 5693    if (nbStripes != 0) {
[](undefined) 5694        uint64_t *xacc = (uint64_t *)acc;
[](undefined) 5695        const uint64_t *xinput = (const uint64_t *)(const void *)input;
[](undefined) 5696        const uint64_t *xsecret = (const uint64_t *)(const void *)secret;
[](undefined) 5697        svuint64_t kSwap = sveor_n_u64_z(svptrue_b64(), svindex_u64(0, 1), 1);
[](undefined) 5698        uint64_t element_count = svcntd();
[](undefined) 5699        if (element_count >= 8) {
[](undefined) 5700            svbool_t mask = svptrue_pat_b64(SV_VL8);
[](undefined) 5701            svuint64_t vacc = svld1_u64(mask, xacc + 0);
[](undefined) 5702            do {
[](undefined) 5703                /* svprfd(svbool_t, void *, enum svfprop); */

[](undefined) 5893XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 5894XXH3_accumulate_512_rvv(  void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 5895                    const void* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 5896                    const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5897{
[](undefined) 5898    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 63) == 0);
[](undefined) 5899    {
[](undefined) 5900        // Try to set vector lenght to 512 bits.
[](undefined) 5901        // If this length is unavailable, then maximum available will be used
[](undefined) 5902        size_t vl = XXH_RVOP(vsetvl_e64m2)(8);
[](undefined) 5903
[](undefined) 5904        uint64_t*       xacc    = (uint64_t*) acc;
[](undefined) 5905        const uint64_t* xinput  = (const uint64_t*) input;
[](undefined) 5906        const uint64_t* xsecret = (const uint64_t*) secret;
[](undefined) 5907        static const uint64_t swap_mask[16] = {1, 0, 3, 2, 5, 4, 7, 6, 9, 8, 11, 10, 13, 12, 15, 14};
[](undefined) 5908        vuint64m2_t xswap_mask = XXH_RVOP(vle64_v_u64m2)(swap_mask, vl);
[](undefined) 5909
[](undefined) 5910        size_t i;
[](undefined) 5911        for (i = 0; i < XXH_STRIPE_LEN/8; i += vl) {
[](undefined) 5912            /* data_vec = xinput[i]; */
[](undefined) 5913            vuint64m2_t data_vec = XXH_RVCAST(u8m2_u64m2)(XXH_RVOP(vle8_v_u8m2)((const uint8_t*)(xinput + i), vl * 8));
[](undefined) 5914            /* key_vec = xsecret[i]; */
[](undefined) 5915            vuint64m2_t key_vec = XXH_RVCAST(u8m2_u64m2)(XXH_RVOP(vle8_v_u8m2)((const uint8_t*)(xsecret + i), vl * 8));
[](undefined) 5916            /* acc_vec = xacc[i]; */
[](undefined) 5917            vuint64m2_t acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vle64_v_u64m2)(xacc + i, vl);
[](undefined) 5918            /* data_key = data_vec ^ key_vec; */
[](undefined) 5919            vuint64m2_t data_key = XXH_RVOP(vxor_vv_u64m2)(data_vec, key_vec, vl);
[](undefined) 5920            /* data_key_hi = data_key >> 32; */
[](undefined) 5921            vuint64m2_t data_key_hi = XXH_RVOP(vsrl_vx_u64m2)(data_key, 32, vl);
[](undefined) 5922            /* data_key_lo = data_key & 0xffffffff; */
[](undefined) 5923            vuint64m2_t data_key_lo = XXH_RVOP(vand_vx_u64m2)(data_key, 0xffffffff, vl);
[](undefined) 5924            /* swap high and low halves */
[](undefined) 5925            vuint64m2_t data_swap = XXH_RVOP(vrgather_vv_u64m2)(data_vec, xswap_mask, vl);
[](undefined) 5926            /* acc_vec += data_key_lo * data_key_hi; */
[](undefined) 5927            acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vmacc_vv_u64m2)(acc_vec, data_key_lo, data_key_hi, vl);
[](undefined) 5928            /* acc_vec += data_swap; */
[](undefined) 5929            acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vadd_vv_u64m2)(acc_vec, data_swap, vl);
[](undefined) 5930            /* xacc[i] = acc_vec; */
[](undefined) 5931            XXH_RVOP(vse64_v_u64m2)(xacc + i, acc_vec, vl);
[](undefined) 5932        }
[](undefined) 5933    }

[](undefined) 5939XXH3_scrambleAcc_rvv(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc, const void* XXH_RESTRICT secret)
[](undefined) 5940{
[](undefined) 5941    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & 15) == 0);
[](undefined) 5942    {
[](undefined) 5943        size_t count = XXH_STRIPE_LEN/8;
[](undefined) 5944        uint64_t* xacc = (uint64_t*)acc;
[](undefined) 5945        const uint8_t* xsecret = (const uint8_t *)secret;
[](undefined) 5946        size_t vl;
[](undefined) 5947        for (; count > 0; count -= vl, xacc += vl, xsecret += vl*8) {
[](undefined) 5948            vl = XXH_RVOP(vsetvl_e64m2)(count);
[](undefined) 5949            {
[](undefined) 5950                /* key_vec = xsecret[i]; */
[](undefined) 5951                vuint64m2_t key_vec = XXH_RVCAST(u8m2_u64m2)(XXH_RVOP(vle8_v_u8m2)(xsecret, vl*8));
[](undefined) 5952                /* acc_vec = xacc[i]; */
[](undefined) 5953                vuint64m2_t acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vle64_v_u64m2)(xacc, vl);
[](undefined) 5954                /* acc_vec ^= acc_vec >> 47; */

[](undefined) 5956                acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vxor_vv_u64m2)(acc_vec, vsrl, vl);
[](undefined) 5957                /* acc_vec ^= key_vec; */
[](undefined) 5958                acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vxor_vv_u64m2)(acc_vec, key_vec, vl);
[](undefined) 5959                /* acc_vec *= XXH_PRIME32_1; */
[](undefined) 5960                acc_vec = XXH_RVOP(vmul_vx_u64m2)(acc_vec, [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f), vl);
[](undefined) 5961                /* xacc[i] *= acc_vec; */

[](undefined) 5963            }
[](undefined) 5964        }

[](undefined) 5968XXH_FORCE_INLINE void

[](undefined) 5970{
[](undefined) 5971    XXH_STATIC_ASSERT(XXH_SEC_ALIGN >= 8);
[](undefined) 5972    XXH_ASSERT(((size_t)customSecret & 7) == 0);
[](undefined) 5973    (void)(&XXH_writeLE64);

[](undefined) 5976        size_t vl;
[](undefined) 5977        size_t VLMAX = XXH_RVOP(vsetvlmax_e64m2)();
[](undefined) 5978        int64_t* cSecret = (int64_t*)customSecret;
[](undefined) 5979        const int64_t* kSecret = (const int64_t*)(const void*)XXH3_kSecret;
[](undefined) 5980
[](undefined) 5981#if __riscv_v_intrinsic >= 1000000
[](undefined) 5982        // ratified v1.0 intrinics version

[](undefined) 5994
