[](undefined) 6009/* scalar variants - universal */
[](undefined) 6010
[](undefined) 6011#if defined(__aarch64__) && (defined(__GNUC__) || defined(__clang__))
[](undefined) 6012/*
[](undefined) 6013 * In XXH3_scalarRound(), GCC and Clang have a similar codegen issue, where they
[](undefined) 6014 * emit an excess mask and a full 64-bit multiply-add (MADD X-form).
[](undefined) 6015 *
[](undefined) 6016 * While this might not seem like much, as AArch64 is a 64-bit architecture, only
[](undefined) 6017 * big Cortex designs have a full 64-bit multiplier.
[](undefined) 6018 *
[](undefined) 6019 * On the little cores, the smaller 32-bit multiplier is used, and full 64-bit
[](undefined) 6020 * multiplies expand to 2-3 multiplies in microcode. This has a major penalty
[](undefined) 6021 * of up to 4 latency cycles and 2 stall cycles in the multiply pipeline.
[](undefined) 6022 *
[](undefined) 6023 * Thankfully, AArch64 still provides the 32-bit long multiply-add (UMADDL) which does
[](undefined) 6024 * not have this penalty and does the mask automatically.
[](undefined) 6025 */
[](undefined) 6026XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64
[](undefined) 6027XXH_mult32to64_add64(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs, xxh_u64 acc)
[](undefined) 6028{
[](undefined) 6029    xxh_u64 ret;
[](undefined) 6030    /* note: %x = 64-bit register, %w = 32-bit register */
[](undefined) 6031    __asm__("umaddl %x0, %w1, %w2, %x3" : "=r" (ret) : "r" (lhs), "r" (rhs), "r" (acc));
[](undefined) 6032    return ret;
[](undefined) 6033}
[](undefined) 6034#else
[](undefined) 6035XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64
[](undefined) 6036XXH_mult32to64_add64(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs, xxh_u64 acc)
[](undefined) 6037{
[](undefined) 6038    return XXH_mult32to64((xxh_u32)lhs, (xxh_u32)rhs) + acc;
[](undefined) 6039}
[](undefined) 6040#endif
[](undefined) 6041
[](undefined) 6049XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 6050XXH3_scalarRound(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 6051                 void const* XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 6052                 void const* XXH_RESTRICT secret,
[](undefined) 6053                 size_t lane)
[](undefined) 6054{
[](undefined) 6055    xxh_u64* xacc = (xxh_u64*) acc;
[](undefined) 6056    xxh_u8 const* xinput  = (xxh_u8 const*) input;
[](undefined) 6057    xxh_u8 const* xsecret = (xxh_u8 const*) secret;
[](undefined) 6058    XXH_ASSERT(lane < XXH_ACC_NB);
[](undefined) 6059    XXH_ASSERT(((size_t)acc & ([XXH_ACC_ALIGN](group__tuning.html#ga15f2f40c9206c691f66cf260841f8ad1)-1)) == 0);
[](undefined) 6060    {
[](undefined) 6061        xxh_u64 const data_val = XXH_readLE64(xinput + lane * 8);
[](undefined) 6062        xxh_u64 const data_key = data_val ^ XXH_readLE64(xsecret + lane * 8);
[](undefined) 6063        xacc[lane ^ 1] += data_val; /* swap adjacent lanes */
[](undefined) 6064        xacc[lane] = XXH_mult32to64_add64(data_key /* & 0xFFFFFFFF */, data_key >> 32, xacc[lane]);
[](undefined) 6065    }
[](undefined) 6066}

[](undefined) 6098XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 6099XXH3_scalarScrambleRound(void* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 6100                         void const* XXH_RESTRICT secret,
[](undefined) 6101                         size_t lane)
[](undefined) 6102{
[](undefined) 6103    xxh_u64* const xacc = (xxh_u64*) acc;   /* presumed aligned */
[](undefined) 6104    const xxh_u8* const xsecret = (const xxh_u8*) secret;   /* no alignment restriction */
[](undefined) 6105    XXH_ASSERT((((size_t)acc) & ([XXH_ACC_ALIGN](group__tuning.html#ga15f2f40c9206c691f66cf260841f8ad1)-1)) == 0);
[](undefined) 6106    XXH_ASSERT(lane < XXH_ACC_NB);
[](undefined) 6107    {
[](undefined) 6108        xxh_u64 const key64 = XXH_readLE64(xsecret + lane * 8);
[](undefined) 6109        xxh_u64 acc64 = xacc[lane];
[](undefined) 6110        acc64 = XXH_xorshift64(acc64, 47);
[](undefined) 6111        acc64 ^= key64;
[](undefined) 6112        acc64 *= [XXH_PRIME32_1](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f);
[](undefined) 6113        xacc[lane] = acc64;
[](undefined) 6114    }

[](undefined) 6130XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 6131XXH3_initCustomSecret_scalar(void* XXH_RESTRICT customSecret, xxh_u64 seed64)
[](undefined) 6132{
[](undefined) 6133    /*
[](undefined) 6134     * We need a separate pointer for the hack below,
[](undefined) 6135     * which requires a non-const pointer.
[](undefined) 6136     * Any decent compiler will optimize this out otherwise.
[](undefined) 6137     */
[](undefined) 6138    const xxh_u8* kSecretPtr = XXH3_kSecret;
[](undefined) 6139    XXH_STATIC_ASSERT((XXH_SECRET_DEFAULT_SIZE & 15) == 0);
[](undefined) 6140
[](undefined) 6141#if defined(__GNUC__) && defined(__aarch64__)
[](undefined) 6142    /*
[](undefined) 6143     * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 6144     * GCC and Clang generate a bunch of MOV/MOVK pairs for aarch64, and they are
[](undefined) 6145     * placed sequentially, in order, at the top of the unrolled loop.
[](undefined) 6146     *
[](undefined) 6147     * While MOVK is great for generating constants (2 cycles for a 64-bit
[](undefined) 6148     * constant compared to 4 cycles for LDR), it fights for bandwidth with
[](undefined) 6149     * the arithmetic instructions.
[](undefined) 6150     *
[](undefined) 6151     *   I   L   S
[](undefined) 6152     * MOVK
[](undefined) 6153     * MOVK
[](undefined) 6154     * MOVK
[](undefined) 6155     * MOVK
[](undefined) 6156     * ADD
[](undefined) 6157     * SUB      STR
[](undefined) 6158     *          STR
[](undefined) 6159     * By forcing loads from memory (as the asm line causes the compiler to assume
[](undefined) 6160     * that XXH3_kSecretPtr has been changed), the pipelines are used more
[](undefined) 6161     * efficiently:
[](undefined) 6162     *   I   L   S
[](undefined) 6163     *      LDR
[](undefined) 6164     *  ADD LDR
[](undefined) 6165     *  SUB     STR
[](undefined) 6166     *          STR
[](undefined) 6167     *
[](undefined) 6168     * See XXH3_NEON_LANES for details on the pipeline.
[](undefined) 6169     *
[](undefined) 6170     * XXH3_64bits_withSeed, len == 256, Snapdragon 835
[](undefined) 6171     *   without hack: 2654.4 MB/s
[](undefined) 6172     *   with hack:    3202.9 MB/s
[](undefined) 6173     */
[](undefined) 6174    XXH_COMPILER_GUARD(kSecretPtr);
[](undefined) 6175#endif
[](undefined) 6176    {   int const nbRounds = XXH_SECRET_DEFAULT_SIZE / 16;
[](undefined) 6177        int i;
[](undefined) 6178        for (i=0; i < nbRounds; i++) {
[](undefined) 6179            /*
[](undefined) 6180             * The asm hack causes the compiler to assume that kSecretPtr aliases with
[](undefined) 6181             * customSecret, and on aarch64, this prevented LDP from merging two
[](undefined) 6182             * loads together for free. Putting the loads together before the stores
[](undefined) 6183             * properly generates LDP.
[](undefined) 6184             */
[](undefined) 6185            xxh_u64 lo = XXH_readLE64(kSecretPtr + 16*i)     + seed64;
[](undefined) 6186            xxh_u64 hi = XXH_readLE64(kSecretPtr + 16*i + 8) - seed64;
[](undefined) 6187            XXH_writeLE64((xxh_u8*)customSecret + 16*i,     lo);
[](undefined) 6188            XXH_writeLE64((xxh_u8*)customSecret + 16*i + 8, hi);
[](undefined) 6189    }   }
[](undefined) 6190}

[](undefined) 6271XXH_FORCE_INLINE void
[](undefined) 6272XXH3_hashLong_internal_loop(xxh_u64* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 6273                      const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT input, size_t len,
[](undefined) 6274                      const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT secret, size_t secretSize,
[](undefined) 6275                            XXH3_f_accumulate f_acc,
[](undefined) 6276                            XXH3_f_scrambleAcc f_scramble)
[](undefined) 6277{
[](undefined) 6278    size_t const nbStripesPerBlock = (secretSize - XXH_STRIPE_LEN) / XXH_SECRET_CONSUME_RATE;
[](undefined) 6279    size_t const block_len = XXH_STRIPE_LEN * nbStripesPerBlock;
[](undefined) 6280    size_t const nb_blocks = (len - 1) / block_len;
[](undefined) 6281
[](undefined) 6282    size_t n;
[](undefined) 6283
[](undefined) 6284    XXH_ASSERT(secretSize >= [XXH3_SECRET_SIZE_MIN](group___x_x_h3__family.html#gaeb79a9111c58af32599f8e5cd71d67d2));
[](undefined) 6285
[](undefined) 6286    for (n = 0; n < nb_blocks; n++) {
[](undefined) 6287        f_acc(acc, input + n*block_len, secret, nbStripesPerBlock);
[](undefined) 6288        f_scramble(acc, secret + secretSize - XXH_STRIPE_LEN);
[](undefined) 6289    }
[](undefined) 6290
[](undefined) 6291    /* last partial block */
[](undefined) 6292    XXH_ASSERT(len > XXH_STRIPE_LEN);
[](undefined) 6293    {   size_t const nbStripes = ((len - 1) - (block_len * nb_blocks)) / XXH_STRIPE_LEN;
[](undefined) 6294        XXH_ASSERT(nbStripes <= (secretSize / XXH_SECRET_CONSUME_RATE));
[](undefined) 6295        f_acc(acc, input + nb_blocks*block_len, secret, nbStripes);
[](undefined) 6296
[](undefined) 6297        /* last stripe */
[](undefined) 6298        {   const xxh_u8* const p = input + len - XXH_STRIPE_LEN;
[](undefined) 6299#define XXH_SECRET_LASTACC_START 7  /* not aligned on 8, last secret is different from acc & scrambler */
[](undefined) 6300            XXH3_accumulate_512(acc, p, secret + secretSize - XXH_STRIPE_LEN - XXH_SECRET_LASTACC_START);
[](undefined) 6301    }   }
[](undefined) 6302}

[](undefined) 6427/*

[](undefined) 6430XXH_NO_INLINE [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 6431XXH3_hashLong_64b_withSeed(const void* XXH_RESTRICT input, size_t len,

[](undefined) 6497/* ===   XXH3 streaming   === */
[](undefined) 6498#ifndef XXH_NO_STREAM
[](undefined) 6499/*
[](undefined) 6500 * Malloc's a pointer that is always aligned to @align.
[](undefined) 6501 *
[](undefined) 6502 * This must be freed with `XXH_alignedFree()`.
[](undefined) 6503 *
[](undefined) 6504 * malloc typically guarantees 16 byte alignment on 64-bit systems and 8 byte
[](undefined) 6505 * alignment on 32-bit. This isn't enough for the 32 byte aligned loads in AVX2
[](undefined) 6506 * or on 32-bit, the 16 byte aligned loads in SSE2 and NEON.
[](undefined) 6507 *
[](undefined) 6508 * This underalignment previously caused a rather obvious crash which went
[](undefined) 6509 * completely unnoticed due to XXH3_createState() not actually being tested.
[](undefined) 6510 * Credit to RedSpah for noticing this bug.
[](undefined) 6511 *
[](undefined) 6512 * The alignment is done manually: Functions like posix_memalign or _mm_malloc
[](undefined) 6513 * are avoided: To maintain portability, we would have to write a fallback
[](undefined) 6514 * like this anyways, and besides, testing for the existence of library
[](undefined) 6515 * functions without relying on external build tools is impossible.
[](undefined) 6516 *
[](undefined) 6517 * The method is simple: Overallocate, manually align, and store the offset
[](undefined) 6518 * to the original behind the returned pointer.
[](undefined) 6519 *
[](undefined) 6520 * Align must be a power of 2 and 8 <= align <= 128.
[](undefined) 6521 */
[](undefined) 6522static XXH_MALLOCF void* XXH_alignedMalloc(size_t s, size_t align)
[](undefined) 6523{
[](undefined) 6524    XXH_ASSERT(align <= 128 && align >= 8); /* range check */
[](undefined) 6525    XXH_ASSERT((align & (align-1)) == 0);   /* power of 2 */
[](undefined) 6526    XXH_ASSERT(s != 0 && s < (s + align));  /* empty/overflow */
[](undefined) 6527    {   /* Overallocate to make room for manual realignment and an offset byte */
[](undefined) 6528        xxh_u8* base = (xxh_u8*)XXH_malloc(s + align);
[](undefined) 6529        if (base != NULL) {
[](undefined) 6530            /*
[](undefined) 6531             * Get the offset needed to align this pointer.
[](undefined) 6532             *
[](undefined) 6533             * Even if the returned pointer is aligned, there will always be
[](undefined) 6534             * at least one byte to store the offset to the original pointer.
[](undefined) 6535             */
[](undefined) 6536            size_t offset = align - ((size_t)base & (align - 1)); /* base % align */
[](undefined) 6537            /* Add the offset for the now-aligned pointer */
[](undefined) 6538            xxh_u8* ptr = base + offset;
[](undefined) 6539
[](undefined) 6540            XXH_ASSERT((size_t)ptr % align == 0);
[](undefined) 6541
[](undefined) 6542            /* Store the offset immediately before the returned pointer. */
[](undefined) 6543            ptr[-1] = (xxh_u8)offset;
[](undefined) 6544            return ptr;
[](undefined) 6545        }
[](undefined) 6546        return NULL;
[](undefined) 6547    }
[](undefined) 6548}
[](undefined) 6549/*
[](undefined) 6550 * Frees an aligned pointer allocated by XXH_alignedMalloc(). Don't pass
[](undefined) 6551 * normal malloc'd pointers, XXH_alignedMalloc has a specific data layout.
[](undefined) 6552 */
[](undefined) 6553static void XXH_alignedFree(void* p)
[](undefined) 6554{
[](undefined) 6555    if (p != NULL) {
[](undefined) 6556        xxh_u8* ptr = (xxh_u8*)p;
[](undefined) 6557        /* Get the offset byte we added in XXH_malloc. */
[](undefined) 6558        xxh_u8 offset = ptr[-1];
[](undefined) 6559        /* Free the original malloc'd pointer */
[](undefined) 6560        xxh_u8* base = ptr - offset;
[](undefined) 6561        XXH_free(base);
[](undefined) 6562    }
[](undefined) 6563}
[](undefined)[ 6575](group___x_x_h3__family.html#ga95592969a08becc37428dd547c36b8fd)[XXH_PUBLIC_API](group__public.html#ga5832897ed49938aefa629a76034b36b1) [XXH3_state_t](group___x_x_h3__family.html#ga8e454084a62efb09fad8ba3cc1c1b1d0)* [XXH3_createState](group___x_x_h3__family.html#ga95592969a08becc37428dd547c36b8fd)(void)

[](undefined) 6678
[](undefined) 6696XXH_FORCE_INLINE const xxh_u8 *
[](undefined) 6697XXH3_consumeStripes(xxh_u64* XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 6698                    size_t* XXH_RESTRICT nbStripesSoFarPtr, size_t nbStripesPerBlock,
[](undefined) 6699                    const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT input, size_t nbStripes,
[](undefined) 6700                    const xxh_u8* XXH_RESTRICT secret, size_t secretLimit,
[](undefined) 6701                    XXH3_f_accumulate f_acc,
[](undefined) 6702                    XXH3_f_scrambleAcc f_scramble)
[](undefined) 6703{
[](undefined) 6704    const xxh_u8* initialSecret = secret + *nbStripesSoFarPtr * XXH_SECRET_CONSUME_RATE;
[](undefined) 6705    /* Process full blocks */
[](undefined) 6706    if (nbStripes >= (nbStripesPerBlock - *nbStripesSoFarPtr)) {
[](undefined) 6707        /* Process the initial partial block... */
[](undefined) 6708        size_t nbStripesThisIter = nbStripesPerBlock - *nbStripesSoFarPtr;
[](undefined) 6709
[](undefined) 6710        do {
[](undefined) 6711            /* Accumulate and scramble */
[](undefined) 6712            f_acc(acc, input, initialSecret, nbStripesThisIter);
[](undefined) 6713            f_scramble(acc, secret + secretLimit);
[](undefined) 6714            input += nbStripesThisIter * XXH_STRIPE_LEN;
[](undefined) 6715            nbStripes -= nbStripesThisIter;
[](undefined) 6716            /* Then continue the loop with the full block size */
[](undefined) 6717            nbStripesThisIter = nbStripesPerBlock;
[](undefined) 6718            initialSecret = secret;
[](undefined) 6719        } while (nbStripes >= nbStripesPerBlock);
[](undefined) 6720        *nbStripesSoFarPtr = 0;
[](undefined) 6721    }
[](undefined) 6722    /* Process a partial block */
[](undefined) 6723    if (nbStripes > 0) {
[](undefined) 6724        f_acc(acc, input, initialSecret, nbStripes);
[](undefined) 6725        input += nbStripes * XXH_STRIPE_LEN;
[](undefined) 6726        *nbStripesSoFarPtr += nbStripes;
[](undefined) 6727    }
[](undefined) 6728    /* Return end pointer */
[](undefined) 6729    return input;
[](undefined) 6730}

[](undefined) 6893
[](undefined) 6894/* ==========================================
[](undefined) 6895 * XXH3 128 bits (a.k.a XXH128)
[](undefined) 6896 * ==========================================
[](undefined) 6897 * XXH3's 128-bit variant has better mixing and strength than the 64-bit variant,
[](undefined) 6898 * even without counting the significantly larger output size.
[](undefined) 6899 *
[](undefined) 6900 * For example, extra steps are taken to mitigate the seed-dependent collisions
[](undefined) 6901 * in 17-240 byte inputs (See XXH3_mix16B and XXH128_mix32B). Note that these
[](undefined) 6902 * steps only mitigate, they do not eliminate the issue: see the DISCLAIMER
[](undefined) 6903 * above XXH3_mix16B() for the cases that get through.
[](undefined) 6904 *
[](undefined) 6905 * This strength naturally comes at the cost of some speed, especially on short
[](undefined) 6906 * lengths. Note that longer hashes are about as fast as the 64-bit version
[](undefined) 6907 * due to it using only a slight modification of the 64-bit loop.
[](undefined) 6908 *
[](undefined) 6909 * XXH128 is also more oriented towards 64-bit machines. It is still extremely
[](undefined) 6910 * fast for a _128-bit_ hash on 32-bit (it usually clears XXH64).
[](undefined) 6911 */
[](undefined) 6912
[](undefined) 6913XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html)
[](undefined) 6914XXH3_len_1to3_128b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 6915{
[](undefined) 6916    /* A doubled version of 1to3_64b with different constants. */
[](undefined) 6917    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 6918    XXH_ASSERT(1 <= len && len <= 3);
[](undefined) 6919    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 6920    /*
[](undefined) 6921     * len = 1: combinedl = { input[0], 0x01, input[0], input[0] }
[](undefined) 6922     * len = 2: combinedl = { input[1], 0x02, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 6923     * len = 3: combinedl = { input[2], 0x03, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 6924     */
[](undefined) 6925    {   xxh_u8 const c1 = input[0];
[](undefined) 6926        xxh_u8 const c2 = input[len >> 1];
[](undefined) 6927        xxh_u8 const c3 = input[len - 1];
[](undefined) 6928        xxh_u32 const combinedl = ((xxh_u32)c1 <<16) | ((xxh_u32)c2 << 24)
[](undefined) 6929                                | ((xxh_u32)c3 << 0) | ((xxh_u32)len << 8);
[](undefined) 6930        xxh_u32 const combinedh = XXH_rotl32(XXH_swap32(combinedl), 13);
[](undefined) 6931        xxh_u64 const bitflipl = (XXH_readLE32(secret) ^ XXH_readLE32(secret+4)) + seed;
[](undefined) 6932        xxh_u64 const bitfliph = (XXH_readLE32(secret+8) ^ XXH_readLE32(secret+12)) - seed;
[](undefined) 6933        xxh_u64 const keyed_lo = (xxh_u64)combinedl ^ bitflipl;
[](undefined) 6934        xxh_u64 const keyed_hi = (xxh_u64)combinedh ^ bitfliph;
[](undefined) 6935        [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) h128;
[](undefined) 6936        h128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = XXH64_avalanche(keyed_lo);
[](undefined) 6937        h128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = XXH64_avalanche(keyed_hi);
[](undefined) 6938        return h128;
[](undefined) 6939    }
[](undefined) 6940}
[](undefined) 6941
[](undefined) 6942XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html)
[](undefined) 6943XXH3_len_4to8_128b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 6944{
[](undefined) 6945    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 6946    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 6947    XXH_ASSERT(4 <= len && len <= 8);
[](undefined) 6948    seed ^= (xxh_u64)XXH_swap32((xxh_u32)seed) << 32;
[](undefined) 6949    {   xxh_u32 const input_lo = XXH_readLE32(input);
[](undefined) 6950        xxh_u32 const input_hi = XXH_readLE32(input + len - 4);
[](undefined) 6951        xxh_u64 const input_64 = input_lo + ((xxh_u64)input_hi << 32);
[](undefined) 6952        xxh_u64 const bitflip = (XXH_readLE64(secret+16) ^ XXH_readLE64(secret+24)) + seed;
[](undefined) 6953        xxh_u64 const keyed = input_64 ^ bitflip;
[](undefined) 6954
[](undefined) 6955        /* Shift len to the left to ensure it is even, this avoids even multiplies. */
[](undefined) 6956        [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) m128 = XXH_mult64to128(keyed, [XXH_PRIME64_1](group___x_x_h64__impl.html#ga20651a9ad53cae6f0a4feb1393d5a716) + (len << 2));
[](undefined) 6957
[](undefined) 6958        m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) += (m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef) << 1);
[](undefined) 6959        m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  ^= (m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) >> 3);
[](undefined) 6960
[](undefined) 6961        m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)   = XXH_xorshift64(m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef), 35);

[](undefined) 6963        m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)   = XXH_xorshift64(m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef), 28);

[](undefined) 6968
[](undefined) 6969XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html)
[](undefined) 6970XXH3_len_9to16_128b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](group__public.html#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 6971{
[](undefined) 6972    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 6973    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 6974    XXH_ASSERT(9 <= len && len <= 16);
[](undefined) 6975    {   xxh_u64 const bitflipl = (XXH_readLE64(secret+32) ^ XXH_readLE64(secret+40)) - seed;
[](undefined) 6976        xxh_u64 const bitfliph = (XXH_readLE64(secret+48) ^ XXH_readLE64(secret+56)) + seed;
[](undefined) 6977        xxh_u64 const input_lo = XXH_readLE64(input);
[](undefined) 6978        xxh_u64       input_hi = XXH_readLE64(input + len - 8);
[](undefined) 6979        [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) m128 = XXH_mult64to128(input_lo ^ input_hi ^ bitflipl, [XXH_PRIME64_1](group___x_x_h64__impl.html#ga20651a9ad53cae6f0a4feb1393d5a716));
[](undefined) 6980        /*
[](undefined) 6981         * Put len in the middle of m128 to ensure that the length gets mixed to
[](undefined) 6982         * both the low and high bits in the 128x64 multiply below.
[](undefined) 6983         */
[](undefined) 6984        m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef) += (xxh_u64)(len - 1) << 54;
[](undefined) 6985        input_hi   ^= bitfliph;
[](undefined) 6986        /*
[](undefined) 6987         * Add the high 32 bits of input_hi to the high 32 bits of m128, then
[](undefined) 6988         * add the long product of the low 32 bits of input_hi and XXH_PRIME32_2 to
[](undefined) 6989         * the high 64 bits of m128.
[](undefined) 6990         *
[](undefined) 6991         * The best approach to this operation is different on 32-bit and 64-bit.
[](undefined) 6992         */
[](undefined) 6993        if (sizeof(void *) < sizeof(xxh_u64)) { /* 32-bit */
[](undefined) 6994            /*
[](undefined) 6995             * 32-bit optimized version, which is more readable.
[](undefined) 6996             *
[](undefined) 6997             * On 32-bit, it removes an ADC and delays a dependency between the two
[](undefined) 6998             * halves of m128.high64, but it generates an extra mask on 64-bit.
[](undefined) 6999             */
[](undefined) 7000            m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) += (input_hi & 0xFFFFFFFF00000000ULL) + XXH_mult32to64((xxh_u32)input_hi, [XXH_PRIME32_2](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79));
[](undefined) 7001        } else {
[](undefined) 7002            /*
[](undefined) 7003             * 64-bit optimized (albeit more confusing) version.
[](undefined) 7004             *
[](undefined) 7005             * Uses some properties of addition and multiplication to remove the mask:
[](undefined) 7006             *
[](undefined) 7007             * Let:
[](undefined) 7008             *    a = input_hi.lo = (input_hi & 0x00000000FFFFFFFF)
[](undefined) 7009             *    b = input_hi.hi = (input_hi & 0xFFFFFFFF00000000)
[](undefined) 7010             *    c = XXH_PRIME32_2
[](undefined) 7011             *
[](undefined) 7012             *    a + (b * c)
[](undefined) 7013             * Inverse Property: x + y - x == y
[](undefined) 7014             *    a + (b * (1 + c - 1))
[](undefined) 7015             * Distributive Property: x * (y + z) == (x * y) + (x * z)
[](undefined) 7016             *    a + (b * 1) + (b * (c - 1))
[](undefined) 7017             * Identity Property: x * 1 == x
[](undefined) 7018             *    a + b + (b * (c - 1))
[](undefined) 7019             *
[](undefined) 7020             * Substitute a, b, and c:
[](undefined) 7021             *    input_hi.hi + input_hi.lo + ((xxh_u64)input_hi.lo * (XXH_PRIME32_2 - 1))
[](undefined) 7022             *
[](undefined) 7023             * Since input_hi.hi + input_hi.lo == input_hi, we get this:
[](undefined) 7024             *    input_hi + ((xxh_u64)input_hi.lo * (XXH_PRIME32_2 - 1))
[](undefined) 7025             */
[](undefined) 7026            m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) += input_hi + XXH_mult32to64((xxh_u32)input_hi, [XXH_PRIME32_2](group___x_x_h32__impl.html#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79) - 1);
[](undefined) 7027        }
[](undefined) 7028        /* m128 ^= XXH_swap64(m128 >> 64); */
[](undefined) 7029        m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  ^= XXH_swap64(m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8));
[](undefined) 7030
[](undefined) 7031        {   /* 128x64 multiply: h128 = m128 * XXH_PRIME64_2; */
[](undefined) 7032            [XXH128_hash_t](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html) h128 = XXH_mult64to128(m128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef), [XXH_PRIME64_2](group___x_x_h64__impl.html#ga33a01ca165b68cef76da009a54e3ba54));
[](undefined) 7033            h128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) += m128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) * [XXH_PRIME64_2](group___x_x_h64__impl.html#ga33a01ca165b68cef76da009a54e3ba54);
[](undefined) 7034
[](undefined) 7035            h128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)   = XXH3_avalanche(h128.[low64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef));
[](undefined) 7036            h128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8)  = XXH3_avalanche(h128.[high64](struct_x_x_h128__hash__t.html#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8));
[](undefined) 7037            return h128;
[](undefined) 7038    }   }

[](undefined)  350   /*
[](undefined)  351    * This part deals with the special case where a unit wants to inline xxHash,
[](undefined)  352    * but "xxhash.h" has previously been included without XXH_INLINE_ALL,
[](undefined)  353    * such as part of some previously included *.h header file.
[](undefined)  354    * Without further action, the new include would just be ignored,
[](undefined)  355    * and functions would effectively _not_ be inlined (silent failure).
[](undefined)  356    * The following macros solve this situation by prefixing all inlined names,
[](undefined)  357    * avoiding naming collision with previous inclusions.
[](undefined)  358    */
[](undefined)  359   /* Before that, we unconditionally #undef all symbols,
[](undefined)  360    * in case they were already defined with XXH_NAMESPACE.

[](undefined)  362    */

[](undefined) 2064
[](undefined) 2065/*-**********************************************************************
[](undefined) 2066 * xxHash implementation
[](undefined) 2067 *-**********************************************************************
[](undefined) 2068 * xxHash's implementation used to be hosted inside xxhash.c.
[](undefined) 2069 *
[](undefined) 2070 * However, inlining requires implementation to be visible to the compiler,
[](undefined) 2071 * hence be included alongside the header.
[](undefined) 2072 * Previously, implementation was hosted inside xxhash.c,
[](undefined) 2073 * which was then #included when inlining was activated.
[](undefined) 2074 * This construction created issues with a few build and install systems,
[](undefined) 2075 * as it required xxhash.c to be stored in /include directory.
[](undefined) 2076 *
[](undefined) 2077 * xxHash implementation is now directly integrated within xxhash.h.
[](undefined) 2078 * As a consequence, xxhash.c is no longer needed in /include.
[](undefined) 2079 *
[](undefined) 2080 * xxhash.c is still available and is still useful.
[](undefined) 2081 * In a "normal" setup, when xxhash is not inlined,
[](undefined) 2082 * xxhash.h only exposes the prototypes and public symbols,
[](undefined) 2083 * while xxhash.c can be built into an object file xxhash.o
[](undefined) 2084 * which can then be linked into the final binary.
[](undefined) 2085 ************************************************************************/
[](undefined) 2086
[](undefined) 2087#if ( defined(XXH_INLINE_ALL) || defined(XXH_PRIVATE_API) \
[](undefined) 2088   || defined(XXH_IMPLEMENTATION) ) && !defined(XXH_IMPLEM_13a8737387)
[](undefined) 2089#  define XXH_IMPLEM_13a8737387
[](undefined) 2090
[](undefined) 2091/* *************************************
[](undefined) 2092*  Tuning parameters
[](undefined) 2093***************************************/
[](undefined) 2094

[](undefined) 2637 * We actually directly use XXH_readLE32 and XXH_readBE32.
[](undefined) 2638 */

[](undefined) 2640
[](undefined) 2641/*
[](undefined) 2642 * Force direct memory access. Only works on CPU which support unaligned memory
[](undefined) 2643 * access in hardware.
[](undefined) 2644 */
[](undefined) 2645static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* memPtr) { return *(const xxh_u32*) memPtr; }
[](undefined) 2646
[](undefined) 2647#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==1))
[](undefined) 2648
[](undefined) 2649/*
[](undefined) 2650 * __attribute__((aligned(1))) is supported by gcc and clang. Originally the
[](undefined) 2651 * documentation claimed that it only increased the alignment, but actually it
[](undefined) 2652 * can decrease it on gcc, clang, and icc:
[](undefined) 2653 * https://gcc.gnu.org/bugzilla/show_bug.cgi?id=69502,
[](undefined) 2654 * https://gcc.godbolt.org/z/xYez1j67Y.
[](undefined) 2655 */
[](undefined) 2656#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 2657typedef union { xxh_u32 u32; } __attribute__((__packed__)) unalign;
[](undefined) 2658#endif
[](undefined) 2659static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* ptr)
[](undefined) 2660{
[](undefined) 2661    typedef __attribute__((__aligned__(1))) __attribute__((__may_alias__)) xxh_u32 xxh_unalign32;
[](undefined) 2662    return *((const xxh_unalign32*)ptr);
[](undefined) 2663}
[](undefined) 2664
[](undefined) 2665#else
[](undefined) 2666
[](undefined) 2667/*
[](undefined) 2668 * Portable and safe solution. Generally efficient.
[](undefined) 2669 * see: https://fastcompression.blogspot.com/2015/08/accessing-unaligned-memory.html
[](undefined) 2670 */
[](undefined) 2671static xxh_u32 XXH_read32(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 2672{
[](undefined) 2673    xxh_u32 val;
[](undefined) 2674    XXH_memcpy(&val, memPtr, sizeof(val));
[](undefined) 2675    return val;
[](undefined) 2676}

[](undefined) 2749 * Once it has been implemented reliably we can add it as an
[](undefined) 2750 * additional case:
[](undefined) 2751 *
[](undefined) 2752 * ```
[](undefined) 2753 * #if defined(__STDC_VERSION__) && (__STDC_VERSION__ >= 202311L)
[](undefined) 2754 * #  include <stddef.h>
[](undefined) 2755 * #  ifdef unreachable
[](undefined) 2756 * #    define XXH_UNREACHABLE() unreachable()
[](undefined) 2757 * #  endif
[](undefined) 2758 * #endif
[](undefined) 2759 * ```
[](undefined) 2760 *
[](undefined) 2761 * Note C++23 also has std::unreachable() which can be detected
[](undefined) 2762 * as follows:
[](undefined) 2763 * ```
[](undefined) 2764 * #if defined(__cpp_lib_unreachable) && (__cpp_lib_unreachable >= 202202L)
[](undefined) 2765 * #  include <utility>
[](undefined) 2766 * #  define XXH_UNREACHABLE() std::unreachable()
[](undefined) 2767 * #endif
[](undefined) 2768 * ```
[](undefined) 2769 * NB: `__cpp_lib_unreachable` is defined in the `<version>` header.
[](undefined) 2770 * We don't use that as including `<utility>` in `extern "C"` blocks
[](undefined) 2771 * doesn't work on GCC12
[](undefined) 2772 */
[](undefined) 2773
[](undefined) 2774#if XXH_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_unreachable)
[](undefined) 2775#  define XXH_UNREACHABLE() __builtin_unreachable()
[](undefined) 2776

[](undefined) 2778#  define XXH_UNREACHABLE() __assume(0)
[](undefined) 2779
[](undefined) 2780#else

[](undefined) 2947static xxh_u32 XXH32_round(xxh_u32 acc, xxh_u32 input)
[](undefined) 2948{
[](undefined) 2949    acc += input * [XXH_PRIME32_2](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/13-group___x_x_h32__impl/#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79);
[](undefined) 2950    acc  = XXH_rotl32(acc, 13);
[](undefined) 2951    acc *= [XXH_PRIME32_1](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/13-group___x_x_h32__impl/#ga29fede2b86ee758448c55edf6171e11f);
[](undefined) 2952#if (defined(__SSE4_1__) || defined(__aarch64__) || defined(__wasm_simd128__)) && !defined(XXH_ENABLE_AUTOVECTORIZE)
[](undefined) 2953    /*
[](undefined) 2954     * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 2955     * A compiler fence is used to prevent GCC and Clang from
[](undefined) 2956     * autovectorizing the XXH32 loop (pragmas and attributes don't work for some
[](undefined) 2957     * reason) without globally disabling SSE4.1.
[](undefined) 2958     *
[](undefined) 2959     * The reason we want to avoid vectorization is because despite working on
[](undefined) 2960     * 4 integers at a time, there are multiple factors slowing XXH32 down on
[](undefined) 2961     * SSE4:
[](undefined) 2962     * - There's a ridiculous amount of lag from pmulld (10 cycles of latency on
[](undefined) 2963     *   newer chips!) making it slightly slower to multiply four integers at
[](undefined) 2964     *   once compared to four integers independently. Even when pmulld was
[](undefined) 2965     *   fastest, Sandy/Ivy Bridge, it is still not worth it to go into SSE
[](undefined) 2966     *   just to multiply unless doing a long operation.
[](undefined) 2967     *
[](undefined) 2968     * - Four instructions are required to rotate,
[](undefined) 2969     *      movqda tmp,  v // not required with VEX encoding
[](undefined) 2970     *      pslld  tmp, 13 // tmp <<= 13
[](undefined) 2971     *      psrld  v,   19 // x >>= 19
[](undefined) 2972     *      por    v,  tmp // x |= tmp
[](undefined) 2973     *   compared to one for scalar:
[](undefined) 2974     *      roll   v, 13    // reliably fast across the board
[](undefined) 2975     *      shldl  v, v, 13 // Sandy Bridge and later prefer this for some reason
[](undefined) 2976     *
[](undefined) 2977     * - Instruction level parallelism is actually more beneficial here because
[](undefined) 2978     *   the SIMD actually serializes this operation: While v1 is rotating, v2
[](undefined) 2979     *   can load data, while v3 can multiply. SSE forces them to operate
[](undefined) 2980     *   together.
[](undefined) 2981     *
[](undefined) 2982     * This is also enabled on AArch64, as Clang is *very aggressive* in vectorizing
[](undefined) 2983     * the loop. NEON is only faster on the A53, and with the newer cores, it is less
[](undefined) 2984     * than half the speed.
[](undefined) 2985     *
[](undefined) 2986     * Additionally, this is used on WASM SIMD128 because it JITs to the same
[](undefined) 2987     * SIMD instructions and has the same issue.
[](undefined) 2988     */
[](undefined) 2989    XXH_COMPILER_GUARD(acc);
[](undefined) 2990#endif
[](undefined) 2991    return acc;
[](undefined) 2992}
[](undefined) 2993
[](undefined) 3004static xxh_u32 XXH32_avalanche(xxh_u32 hash)
[](undefined) 3005{
[](undefined) 3006    hash ^= hash >> 15;
[](undefined) 3007    hash *= [XXH_PRIME32_2](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/13-group___x_x_h32__impl/#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79);
[](undefined) 3008    hash ^= hash >> 13;
[](undefined) 3009    hash *= [XXH_PRIME32_3](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/13-group___x_x_h32__impl/#gacdda6ef95561d39cf2f9b7f2cd5c6693);
[](undefined) 3010    hash ^= hash >> 16;
[](undefined) 3011    return hash;
[](undefined) 3012}

[](undefined) 3025    acc[1] = seed + [XXH_PRIME32_2](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/13-group___x_x_h32__impl/#ga23d49e684c0551afaed8e4ff40940c79);

[](undefined) 3036XXH_FORCE_INLINE const xxh_u8 *
[](undefined) 3037XXH32_consumeLong(
[](undefined) 3038    xxh_u32 *XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 3039    xxh_u8 const *XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 3040    size_t len,
[](undefined) 3041    [XXH_alignment](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/19-group__impl/#gadec56fccd4f83e34902b6b09ecccfff5) align
[](undefined) 3042)
[](undefined) 3043{
[](undefined) 3044    const xxh_u8* const bEnd = input + len;
[](undefined) 3045    const xxh_u8* const limit = bEnd - 15;
[](undefined) 3046    XXH_ASSERT(acc != NULL);
[](undefined) 3047    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 3048    XXH_ASSERT(len >= 16);
[](undefined) 3049    do {
[](undefined) 3050        acc[0] = XXH32_round(acc[0], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;
[](undefined) 3051        acc[1] = XXH32_round(acc[1], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;
[](undefined) 3052        acc[2] = XXH32_round(acc[2], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;
[](undefined) 3053        acc[3] = XXH32_round(acc[3], XXH_get32bits(input)); input += 4;
[](undefined) 3054    } while (input < limit);
[](undefined) 3055
[](undefined) 3056    return input;
[](undefined) 3057}

[](undefined) 3086static XXH_PUREF xxh_u32

[](undefined) 3090    hash += (*ptr++) * XXH_PRIME32_5;                 \

[](undefined) 3102    /* Compact rerolled version; generally faster */
[](undefined) 3103    if (![XXH32_ENDJMP](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/21-group__tuning/#gad5824529c4ad74d2d5cc05bf4868613a)) {

[](undefined) 3108        }

[](undefined) 3112        }
[](undefined) 3113        return XXH32_avalanche(hash);

[](undefined) 3118           case 8:       XXH_PROCESS4;

[](undefined) 3122

[](undefined) 3125           case 9:       XXH_PROCESS4;

[](undefined) 3130
[](undefined) 3131           case 14:      XXH_PROCESS4;

[](undefined) 3133           case 10:      XXH_PROCESS4;

[](undefined) 3339/* *******************************************************************
[](undefined) 3340*  64-bit hash functions
[](undefined) 3341*********************************************************************/
[](undefined) 3347/*******   Memory access   *******/
[](undefined) 3348
[](undefined) 3349typedef [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) xxh_u64;
[](undefined) 3350
[](undefined) 3351#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 3352#  define U64 xxh_u64
[](undefined) 3353#endif
[](undefined) 3354
[](undefined) 3355#if (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==3))
[](undefined) 3356/*
[](undefined) 3357 * Manual byteshift. Best for old compilers which don't inline memcpy.
[](undefined) 3358 * We actually directly use XXH_readLE64 and XXH_readBE64.
[](undefined) 3359 */
[](undefined) 3360#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==2))
[](undefined) 3361
[](undefined) 3362/* Force direct memory access. Only works on CPU which support unaligned memory access in hardware */
[](undefined) 3363static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3364{
[](undefined) 3365    return *(const xxh_u64*) memPtr;
[](undefined) 3366}
[](undefined) 3367
[](undefined) 3368#elif (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==1))
[](undefined) 3369
[](undefined) 3370/*
[](undefined) 3371 * __attribute__((aligned(1))) is supported by gcc and clang. Originally the
[](undefined) 3372 * documentation claimed that it only increased the alignment, but actually it
[](undefined) 3373 * can decrease it on gcc, clang, and icc:
[](undefined) 3374 * https://gcc.gnu.org/bugzilla/show_bug.cgi?id=69502,
[](undefined) 3375 * https://gcc.godbolt.org/z/xYez1j67Y.
[](undefined) 3376 */
[](undefined) 3377#ifdef XXH_OLD_NAMES
[](undefined) 3378typedef union { xxh_u32 u32; xxh_u64 u64; } __attribute__((__packed__)) unalign64;
[](undefined) 3379#endif
[](undefined) 3380static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* ptr)
[](undefined) 3381{
[](undefined) 3382    typedef __attribute__((__aligned__(1))) __attribute__((__may_alias__)) xxh_u64 xxh_unalign64;
[](undefined) 3383    return *((const xxh_unalign64*)ptr);
[](undefined) 3384}
[](undefined) 3385
[](undefined) 3386#else
[](undefined) 3387
[](undefined) 3388/*
[](undefined) 3389 * Portable and safe solution. Generally efficient.
[](undefined) 3390 * see: https://fastcompression.blogspot.com/2015/08/accessing-unaligned-memory.html
[](undefined) 3391 */
[](undefined) 3392static xxh_u64 XXH_read64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3393{
[](undefined) 3394    xxh_u64 val;
[](undefined) 3395    XXH_memcpy(&val, memPtr, sizeof(val));
[](undefined) 3396    return val;
[](undefined) 3397}
[](undefined) 3398
[](undefined) 3399#endif   /* XXH_FORCE_DIRECT_MEMORY_ACCESS */
[](undefined) 3400
[](undefined) 3401#if defined(_MSC_VER)     /* Visual Studio */
[](undefined) 3402#  define XXH_swap64 _byteswap_uint64
[](undefined) 3403#elif XXH_GCC_VERSION >= 403
[](undefined) 3404#  define XXH_swap64 __builtin_bswap64
[](undefined) 3405#else
[](undefined) 3406static xxh_u64 XXH_swap64(xxh_u64 x)
[](undefined) 3407{
[](undefined) 3408    return  ((x << 56) & 0xff00000000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3409            ((x << 40) & 0x00ff000000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3410            ((x << 24) & 0x0000ff0000000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3411            ((x << 8)  & 0x000000ff00000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3412            ((x >> 8)  & 0x00000000ff000000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3413            ((x >> 24) & 0x0000000000ff0000ULL) |
[](undefined) 3414            ((x >> 40) & 0x000000000000ff00ULL) |
[](undefined) 3415            ((x >> 56) & 0x00000000000000ffULL);
[](undefined) 3416}
[](undefined) 3417#endif
[](undefined) 3418
[](undefined) 3419
[](undefined) 3420/* XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==3 is an endian-independent byteshift load. */
[](undefined) 3421#if (defined(XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS) && (XXH_FORCE_MEMORY_ACCESS==3))
[](undefined) 3422
[](undefined) 3423XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64 XXH_readLE64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3424{
[](undefined) 3425    const xxh_u8* bytePtr = (const xxh_u8 *)memPtr;
[](undefined) 3426    return bytePtr[0]
[](undefined) 3427         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[1] << 8)
[](undefined) 3428         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[2] << 16)
[](undefined) 3429         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[3] << 24)
[](undefined) 3430         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[4] << 32)
[](undefined) 3431         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[5] << 40)
[](undefined) 3432         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[6] << 48)
[](undefined) 3433         | ((xxh_u64)bytePtr[7] << 56);
[](undefined) 3434}
[](undefined) 3435
[](undefined) 3436XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64 XXH_readBE64(const void* memPtr)
[](undefined) 3437{
[](undefined) 3438    const xxh_u8* bytePtr = (const xxh_u8 *)memPtr;

[](undefined) 3563XXH_FORCE_INLINE const xxh_u8 *
[](undefined) 3564XXH64_consumeLong(
[](undefined) 3565    xxh_u64 *XXH_RESTRICT acc,
[](undefined) 3566    xxh_u8 const *XXH_RESTRICT input,
[](undefined) 3567    size_t len,
[](undefined) 3568    [XXH_alignment](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/19-group__impl/#gadec56fccd4f83e34902b6b09ecccfff5) align
[](undefined) 3569)
[](undefined) 3570{
[](undefined) 3571    const xxh_u8* const bEnd = input + len;
[](undefined) 3572    const xxh_u8* const limit = bEnd - 31;
[](undefined) 3573    XXH_ASSERT(acc != NULL);
[](undefined) 3574    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 3575    XXH_ASSERT(len >= 32);
[](undefined) 3576    do {
[](undefined) 3577        /* reroll on 32-bit */
[](undefined) 3578        if (sizeof(void *) < sizeof(xxh_u64)) {
[](undefined) 3579            size_t i;
[](undefined) 3580            for (i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
[](undefined) 3581                acc[i] = XXH64_round(acc[i], XXH_get64bits(input));
[](undefined) 3582                input += 8;
[](undefined) 3583            }
[](undefined) 3584        } else {
[](undefined) 3585            acc[0] = XXH64_round(acc[0], XXH_get64bits(input)); input += 8;
[](undefined) 3586            acc[1] = XXH64_round(acc[1], XXH_get64bits(input)); input += 8;
[](undefined) 3587            acc[2] = XXH64_round(acc[2], XXH_get64bits(input)); input += 8;
[](undefined) 3588            acc[3] = XXH64_round(acc[3], XXH_get64bits(input)); input += 8;
[](undefined) 3589        }
[](undefined) 3590    } while (input < limit);
[](undefined) 3591
[](undefined) 3592    return input;
[](undefined) 3593}

[](undefined) 3599XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF xxh_u64
[](undefined) 3600XXH64_mergeAccs(const xxh_u64 *acc)
[](undefined) 3601{
[](undefined) 3602    XXH_ASSERT(acc != NULL);
[](undefined) 3603    {
[](undefined) 3604        xxh_u64 h64 = XXH_rotl64(acc[0], 1) + XXH_rotl64(acc[1], 7)
[](undefined) 3605                    + XXH_rotl64(acc[2], 12) + XXH_rotl64(acc[3], 18);
[](undefined) 3606        /* reroll on 32-bit */
[](undefined) 3607        if (sizeof(void *) < sizeof(xxh_u64)) {
[](undefined) 3608            size_t i;
[](undefined) 3609            for (i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
[](undefined) 3610                h64 = XXH64_mergeRound(h64, acc[i]);
[](undefined) 3611            }
[](undefined) 3612        } else {
[](undefined) 3613            h64 = XXH64_mergeRound(h64, acc[0]);
[](undefined) 3614            h64 = XXH64_mergeRound(h64, acc[1]);
[](undefined) 3615            h64 = XXH64_mergeRound(h64, acc[2]);
[](undefined) 3616            h64 = XXH64_mergeRound(h64, acc[3]);
[](undefined) 3617        }

[](undefined) 3899/*
[](undefined) 3900 * One goal of XXH3 is to make it fast on both 32-bit and 64-bit, while
[](undefined) 3901 * remaining a true 64-bit/128-bit hash function.
[](undefined) 3902 *
[](undefined) 3903 * This is done by prioritizing a subset of 64-bit operations that can be
[](undefined) 3904 * emulated without too many steps on the average 32-bit machine.
[](undefined) 3905 *
[](undefined) 3906 * For example, these two lines seem similar, and run equally fast on 64-bit:
[](undefined) 3907 *
[](undefined) 3908 *   xxh_u64 x;
[](undefined) 3909 *   x ^= (x >> 47); // good
[](undefined) 3910 *   x ^= (x >> 13); // bad
[](undefined) 3911 *
[](undefined) 3912 * However, to a 32-bit machine, there is a major difference.
[](undefined) 3913 *
[](undefined) 3914 * x ^= (x >> 47) looks like this:
[](undefined) 3915 *
[](undefined) 3916 *   x.lo ^= (x.hi >> (47 - 32));
[](undefined) 3917 *
[](undefined) 3918 * while x ^= (x >> 13) looks like this:
[](undefined) 3919 *
[](undefined) 3920 *   // note: funnel shifts are not usually cheap.
[](undefined) 3921 *   x.lo ^= (x.lo >> 13) | (x.hi << (32 - 13));
[](undefined) 3922 *   x.hi ^= (x.hi >> 13);
[](undefined) 3923 *
[](undefined) 3924 * The first one is significantly faster than the second, simply because the
[](undefined) 3925 * shift is larger than 32. This means:
[](undefined) 3926 *  - All the bits we need are in the upper 32 bits, so we can ignore the lower
[](undefined) 3927 *    32 bits in the shift.
[](undefined) 3928 *  - The shift result will always fit in the lower 32 bits, and therefore,
[](undefined) 3929 *    we can ignore the upper 32 bits in the xor.
[](undefined) 3930 *
[](undefined) 3931 * Thanks to this optimization, XXH3 only requires these features to be efficient:
[](undefined) 3932 *
[](undefined) 3933 *  - Usable unaligned access
[](undefined) 3934 *  - A 32-bit or 64-bit ALU
[](undefined) 3935 *      - If 32-bit, a decent ADC instruction
[](undefined) 3936 *  - A 32 or 64-bit multiply with a 64-bit result
[](undefined) 3937 *  - For the 128-bit variant, a decent byteswap helps short inputs.
[](undefined) 3938 *
[](undefined) 3939 * The first two are already required by XXH32, and almost all 32-bit and 64-bit
[](undefined) 3940 * platforms which can run XXH32 can run XXH3 efficiently.
[](undefined) 3941 *
[](undefined) 3942 * Thumb-1, the classic 16-bit only subset of ARM's instruction set, is one
[](undefined) 3943 * notable exception.
[](undefined) 3944 *
[](undefined) 3945 * First of all, Thumb-1 lacks support for the UMULL instruction which
[](undefined) 3946 * performs the important long multiply. This means numerous __aeabi_lmul
[](undefined) 3947 * calls.
[](undefined) 3948 *
[](undefined) 3949 * Second of all, the 8 functional registers are just not enough.
[](undefined) 3950 * Setup for __aeabi_lmul, byteshift loads, pointers, and all arithmetic need
[](undefined) 3951 * Lo registers, and this shuffling results in thousands more MOVs than A32.
[](undefined) 3952 *
[](undefined) 3953 * A32 and T32 don't have this limitation. They can access all 14 registers,
[](undefined) 3954 * do a 32->64 multiply with UMULL, and the flexible operand allowing free
[](undefined) 3955 * shifts is helpful, too.
[](undefined) 3956 *
[](undefined) 3957 * Therefore, we do a quick sanity check.
[](undefined) 3958 *
[](undefined) 3959 * If compiling Thumb-1 for a target which supports ARM instructions, we will
[](undefined) 3960 * emit a warning, as it is not a "sane" platform to compile for.
[](undefined) 3961 *
[](undefined) 3962 * Usually, if this happens, it is because of an accident and you probably need
[](undefined) 3963 * to specify -march, as you likely meant to compile for a newer architecture.
[](undefined) 3964 *
[](undefined) 3965 * Credit: large sections of the vectorial and asm source code paths
[](undefined) 3966 *         have been contributed by @easyaspi314
[](undefined) 3967 */
[](undefined) 3968#if defined(__thumb__) && !defined(__thumb2__) && defined(__ARM_ARCH_ISA_ARM)
[](undefined) 3969#   warning "XXH3 is highly inefficient without ARM or Thumb-2."
[](undefined) 3970#endif
[](undefined) 3971

[](undefined) 3973 * Vectorization detection
[](undefined) 3974 * ========================================== */
[](undefined) 3975

[](undefined) 4095/*
[](undefined) 4096 * UGLY HACK:
[](undefined) 4097 * GCC usually generates the best code with -O3 for xxHash.
[](undefined) 4098 *
[](undefined) 4099 * However, when targeting AVX2, it is overzealous in its unrolling resulting
[](undefined) 4100 * in code roughly 3/4 the speed of Clang.
[](undefined) 4101 *
[](undefined) 4102 * There are other issues, such as GCC splitting _mm256_loadu_si256 into
[](undefined) 4103 * _mm_loadu_si128 + _mm256_inserti128_si256. This is an optimization which
[](undefined) 4104 * only applies to Sandy and Ivy Bridge... which don't even support AVX2.
[](undefined) 4105 *
[](undefined) 4106 * That is why when compiling the AVX2 version, it is recommended to use either
[](undefined) 4107 *   -O2 -mavx2 -march=haswell
[](undefined) 4108 * or
[](undefined) 4109 *   -O2 -mavx2 -mno-avx256-split-unaligned-load
[](undefined) 4110 * for decent performance, or to use Clang instead.
[](undefined) 4111 *
[](undefined) 4112 * Fortunately, we can control the first one with a pragma that forces GCC into
[](undefined) 4113 * -O2, but the other one we can't control without "failed to inline always
[](undefined) 4114 * inline function due to target mismatch" warnings.
[](undefined) 4115 */
[](undefined) 4116#if XXH_VECTOR == XXH_AVX2 /* AVX2 */ \
[](undefined) 4117  && defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__) /* GCC, not Clang */ \
[](undefined) 4118  && defined(__OPTIMIZE__) && XXH_SIZE_OPT <= 0 /* respect -O0 and -Os */
[](undefined) 4119#  pragma GCC push_options
[](undefined) 4120#  pragma GCC optimize("-O2")
[](undefined) 4121#endif
[](undefined) 4122
[](undefined) 4123#if XXH_VECTOR == XXH_NEON
[](undefined) 4124

[](undefined) 4126 * UGLY HACK: While AArch64 GCC on Linux does not seem to care, on macOS, GCC -O3

[](undefined) 4128 *

[](undefined) 4130 * so the only option is to mark it as aliasing.
[](undefined) 4131 */

[](undefined) 4253#if XXH_VECTOR == XXH_VSX
[](undefined) 4254/* Annoyingly, these headers _may_ define three macros: `bool`, `vector`,
[](undefined) 4255 * and `pixel`. This is a problem for obvious reasons.
[](undefined) 4256 *
[](undefined) 4257 * These keywords are unnecessary; the spec literally says they are
[](undefined) 4258 * equivalent to `__bool`, `__vector`, and `__pixel` and may be undef'd
[](undefined) 4259 * after including the header.
[](undefined) 4260 *
[](undefined) 4261 * We use pragma push_macro/pop_macro to keep the namespace clean. */
[](undefined) 4262#  pragma push_macro("bool")
[](undefined) 4263#  pragma push_macro("vector")
[](undefined) 4264#  pragma push_macro("pixel")
[](undefined) 4265/* silence potential macro redefined warnings */
[](undefined) 4266#  undef bool
[](undefined) 4267#  undef vector
[](undefined) 4268#  undef pixel
[](undefined) 4269
[](undefined) 4270#  if defined(__s390x__)
[](undefined) 4271#    include <s390intrin.h>
[](undefined) 4272#  else
[](undefined) 4273#    include <altivec.h>
[](undefined) 4274#  endif
[](undefined) 4275
[](undefined) 4276/* Restore the original macro values, if applicable. */
[](undefined) 4277#  pragma pop_macro("pixel")
[](undefined) 4278#  pragma pop_macro("vector")
[](undefined) 4279#  pragma pop_macro("bool")
[](undefined) 4280
[](undefined) 4281typedef __vector unsigned long long xxh_u64x2;

[](undefined) 4331/*
[](undefined) 4332 * vec_mulo and vec_mule are very problematic intrinsics on PowerPC
[](undefined) 4333 *
[](undefined) 4334 * These intrinsics weren't added until GCC 8, despite existing for a while,
[](undefined) 4335 * and they are endian dependent. Also, their meaning swap depending on version.
[](undefined) 4336 * */
[](undefined) 4337# if defined(__s390x__)
[](undefined) 4338 /* s390x is always big endian, no issue on this platform */
[](undefined) 4339#  define XXH_vec_mulo vec_mulo
[](undefined) 4340#  define XXH_vec_mule vec_mule
[](undefined) 4341# elif defined(__clang__) && XXH_HAS_BUILTIN(__builtin_altivec_vmuleuw) && !defined(__ibmxl__)
[](undefined) 4342/* Clang has a better way to control this, we can just use the builtin which doesn't swap. */
[](undefined) 4343 /* The IBM XL Compiler (which defined __clang__) only implements the vec_* operations */
[](undefined) 4344#  define XXH_vec_mulo __builtin_altivec_vmulouw
[](undefined) 4345#  define XXH_vec_mule __builtin_altivec_vmuleuw
[](undefined) 4346# else
[](undefined) 4347/* gcc needs inline assembly */
[](undefined) 4348/* Adapted from https://github.com/google/highwayhash/blob/master/highwayhash/hh_vsx.h. */
[](undefined) 4349XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64x2 XXH_vec_mulo(xxh_u32x4 a, xxh_u32x4 b)
[](undefined) 4350{
[](undefined) 4351    xxh_u64x2 result;
[](undefined) 4352    __asm__("vmulouw %0, %1, %2" : "=v" (result) : "v" (a), "v" (b));
[](undefined) 4353    return result;
[](undefined) 4354}
[](undefined) 4355XXH_FORCE_INLINE xxh_u64x2 XXH_vec_mule(xxh_u32x4 a, xxh_u32x4 b)
[](undefined) 4356{
[](undefined) 4357    xxh_u64x2 result;
[](undefined) 4358    __asm__("vmuleuw %0, %1, %2" : "=v" (result) : "v" (a), "v" (b));
[](undefined) 4359    return result;
[](undefined) 4360}
[](undefined) 4361# endif /* XXH_vec_mulo, XXH_vec_mule */
[](undefined) 4362#endif /* XXH_VECTOR == XXH_VSX */

[](undefined) 4453}

[](undefined) 4455#    define XXH_mult32to64(x, y) __emulu((unsigned)(x), (unsigned)(y))
[](undefined) 4456#else

[](undefined) 4458 * Downcast + upcast is usually better than masking on older compilers like
[](undefined) 4459 * GCC 4.2 (especially 32-bit ones), all without affecting newer compilers.
[](undefined) 4460 *
[](undefined) 4461 * The other method, (x & 0xFFFFFFFF) * (y & 0xFFFFFFFF), will AND both operands

[](undefined) 4476static [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/)
[](undefined) 4477XXH_mult64to128(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs)
[](undefined) 4478{
[](undefined) 4479    /*
[](undefined) 4480     * GCC/Clang __uint128_t method.
[](undefined) 4481     *
[](undefined) 4482     * On most 64-bit targets, GCC and Clang define a __uint128_t type.
[](undefined) 4483     * This is usually the best way as it usually uses a native long 64-bit
[](undefined) 4484     * multiply, such as MULQ on x86_64 or MUL + UMULH on aarch64.
[](undefined) 4485     *
[](undefined) 4486     * Usually.
[](undefined) 4487     *
[](undefined) 4488     * Despite being a 32-bit platform, Clang (and emscripten) define this type
[](undefined) 4489     * despite not having the arithmetic for it. This results in a laggy
[](undefined) 4490     * compiler builtin call which calculates a full 128-bit multiply.
[](undefined) 4491     * In that case it is best to use the portable one.
[](undefined) 4492     * https://github.com/Cyan4973/xxHash/issues/211#issuecomment-515575677
[](undefined) 4493     */
[](undefined) 4494#if (defined(__GNUC__) || defined(__clang__)) && !defined(__wasm__) \
[](undefined) 4495    && defined(__SIZEOF_INT128__) \
[](undefined) 4496    || (defined(_INTEGRAL_MAX_BITS) && _INTEGRAL_MAX_BITS >= 128)
[](undefined) 4497
[](undefined) 4498    __uint128_t const product = (__uint128_t)lhs * (__uint128_t)rhs;
[](undefined) 4499    [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/) r128;
[](undefined) 4500    r128.[low64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = (xxh_u64)(product);
[](undefined) 4501    r128.[high64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = (xxh_u64)(product >> 64);
[](undefined) 4502    return r128;
[](undefined) 4503
[](undefined) 4504    /*
[](undefined) 4505     * MSVC for x64's _umul128 method.
[](undefined) 4506     *
[](undefined) 4507     * xxh_u64 _umul128(xxh_u64 Multiplier, xxh_u64 Multiplicand, xxh_u64 *HighProduct);
[](undefined) 4508     *
[](undefined) 4509     * This compiles to single operand MUL on x64.
[](undefined) 4510     */
[](undefined) 4511#elif (defined(_M_X64) || defined(_M_IA64)) && !defined(_M_ARM64EC)
[](undefined) 4512
[](undefined) 4513#ifndef _MSC_VER
[](undefined) 4514#   pragma intrinsic(_umul128)
[](undefined) 4515#endif
[](undefined) 4516    xxh_u64 product_high;
[](undefined) 4517    xxh_u64 const product_low = _umul128(lhs, rhs, &product_high);
[](undefined) 4518    [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/) r128;
[](undefined) 4519    r128.[low64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = product_low;
[](undefined) 4520    r128.[high64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = product_high;
[](undefined) 4521    return r128;
[](undefined) 4522
[](undefined) 4523    /*
[](undefined) 4524     * MSVC for ARM64's __umulh method.
[](undefined) 4525     *
[](undefined) 4526     * This compiles to the same MUL + UMULH as GCC/Clang's __uint128_t method.
[](undefined) 4527     */
[](undefined) 4528#elif defined(_M_ARM64) || defined(_M_ARM64EC)
[](undefined) 4529
[](undefined) 4530#ifndef _MSC_VER
[](undefined) 4531#   pragma intrinsic(__umulh)
[](undefined) 4532#endif
[](undefined) 4533    [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/) r128;
[](undefined) 4534    r128.[low64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = lhs * rhs;
[](undefined) 4535    r128.[high64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = __umulh(lhs, rhs);
[](undefined) 4536    return r128;
[](undefined) 4537
[](undefined) 4538#else
[](undefined) 4539    /*
[](undefined) 4540     * Portable scalar method. Optimized for 32-bit and 64-bit ALUs.
[](undefined) 4541     *
[](undefined) 4542     * This is a fast and simple grade school multiply, which is shown below
[](undefined) 4543     * with base 10 arithmetic instead of base 0x100000000.
[](undefined) 4544     *
[](undefined) 4545     *           9 3 // D2 lhs = 93
[](undefined) 4546     *         x 7 5 // D2 rhs = 75
[](undefined) 4547     *     ----------
[](undefined) 4548     *           1 5 // D2 lo_lo = (93 % 10) * (75 % 10) = 15
[](undefined) 4549     *         4 5 | // D2 hi_lo = (93 / 10) * (75 % 10) = 45
[](undefined) 4550     *         2 1 | // D2 lo_hi = (93 % 10) * (75 / 10) = 21
[](undefined) 4551     *     + 6 3 | | // D2 hi_hi = (93 / 10) * (75 / 10) = 63
[](undefined) 4552     *     ---------
[](undefined) 4553     *         2 7 | // D2 cross = (15 / 10) + (45 % 10) + 21 = 27
[](undefined) 4554     *     + 6 7 | | // D2 upper = (27 / 10) + (45 / 10) + 63 = 67
[](undefined) 4555     *     ---------
[](undefined) 4556     *       6 9 7 5 // D4 res = (27 * 10) + (15 % 10) + (67 * 100) = 6975
[](undefined) 4557     *
[](undefined) 4558     * The reasons for adding the products like this are:
[](undefined) 4559     *  1. It avoids manual carry tracking. Just like how
[](undefined) 4560     *     (9 * 9) + 9 + 9 = 99, the same applies with this for UINT64_MAX.
[](undefined) 4561     *     This avoids a lot of complexity.
[](undefined) 4562     *
[](undefined) 4563     *  2. It hints for, and on Clang, compiles to, the powerful UMAAL
[](undefined) 4564     *     instruction available in ARM's Digital Signal Processing extension
[](undefined) 4565     *     in 32-bit ARMv6 and later, which is shown below:
[](undefined) 4566     *
[](undefined) 4567     *         void UMAAL(xxh_u32 *RdLo, xxh_u32 *RdHi, xxh_u32 Rn, xxh_u32 Rm)
[](undefined) 4568     *         {
[](undefined) 4569     *             xxh_u64 product = (xxh_u64)*RdLo * (xxh_u64)*RdHi + Rn + Rm;
[](undefined) 4570     *             *RdLo = (xxh_u32)(product & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4571     *             *RdHi = (xxh_u32)(product >> 32);
[](undefined) 4572     *         }
[](undefined) 4573     *
[](undefined) 4574     *     This instruction was designed for efficient long multiplication, and
[](undefined) 4575     *     allows this to be calculated in only 4 instructions at speeds
[](undefined) 4576     *     comparable to some 64-bit ALUs.
[](undefined) 4577     *
[](undefined) 4578     *  3. It isn't terrible on other platforms. Usually this will be a couple
[](undefined) 4579     *     of 32-bit ADD/ADCs.
[](undefined) 4580     */
[](undefined) 4581
[](undefined) 4582    /* First calculate all of the cross products. */
[](undefined) 4583    xxh_u64 const lo_lo = XXH_mult32to64(lhs & 0xFFFFFFFF, rhs & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4584    xxh_u64 const hi_lo = XXH_mult32to64(lhs >> 32,        rhs & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4585    xxh_u64 const lo_hi = XXH_mult32to64(lhs & 0xFFFFFFFF, rhs >> 32);
[](undefined) 4586    xxh_u64 const hi_hi = XXH_mult32to64(lhs >> 32,        rhs >> 32);
[](undefined) 4587
[](undefined) 4588    /* Now add the products together. These will never overflow. */
[](undefined) 4589    xxh_u64 const cross = (lo_lo >> 32) + (hi_lo & 0xFFFFFFFF) + lo_hi;
[](undefined) 4590    xxh_u64 const upper = (hi_lo >> 32) + (cross >> 32)        + hi_hi;
[](undefined) 4591    xxh_u64 const lower = (cross << 32) | (lo_lo & 0xFFFFFFFF);
[](undefined) 4592
[](undefined) 4593    [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/) r128;
[](undefined) 4594    r128.[low64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef)  = lower;
[](undefined) 4595    r128.[high64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8) = upper;
[](undefined) 4596    return r128;
[](undefined) 4597#endif
[](undefined) 4598}
[](undefined) 4599
[](undefined) 4610static xxh_u64
[](undefined) 4611XXH3_mul128_fold64(xxh_u64 lhs, xxh_u64 rhs)
[](undefined) 4612{
[](undefined) 4613    [XXH128_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/) product = XXH_mult64to128(lhs, rhs);
[](undefined) 4614    return product.[low64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#a706323c9b3e76414ec77f8f93e8644ef) ^ product.[high64](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/24-struct_x_x_h128__hash__t/#ad4e0c851eb11bd3f8042ceebc15fe0a8);
[](undefined) 4615}

[](undefined) 4620    XXH_ASSERT(0 <= shift && shift < 64);

[](undefined) 4624/*
[](undefined) 4625 * This is a fast avalanche stage,
[](undefined) 4626 * suitable when input bits are already partially mixed
[](undefined) 4627 */
[](undefined) 4628static [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) XXH3_avalanche(xxh_u64 h64)
[](undefined) 4629{
[](undefined) 4630    h64 = XXH_xorshift64(h64, 37);
[](undefined) 4631    h64 *= PRIME_MX1;
[](undefined) 4632    h64 = XXH_xorshift64(h64, 32);
[](undefined) 4633    return h64;
[](undefined) 4634}
[](undefined) 4635
[](undefined) 4636/*
[](undefined) 4637 * This is a stronger avalanche,
[](undefined) 4638 * inspired by Pelle Evensen's rrmxmx
[](undefined) 4639 * preferable when input has not been previously mixed
[](undefined) 4640 */
[](undefined) 4641static [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) XXH3_rrmxmx(xxh_u64 h64, xxh_u64 len)
[](undefined) 4642{
[](undefined) 4643    /* this mix is inspired by Pelle Evensen's rrmxmx */
[](undefined) 4644    h64 ^= XXH_rotl64(h64, 49) ^ XXH_rotl64(h64, 24);
[](undefined) 4645    h64 *= PRIME_MX2;
[](undefined) 4646    h64 ^= (h64 >> 35) + len ;
[](undefined) 4647    h64 *= PRIME_MX2;
[](undefined) 4648    return XXH_xorshift64(h64, 28);
[](undefined) 4649}

[](undefined) 4651
[](undefined) 4652/* ==========================================
[](undefined) 4653 * Short keys
[](undefined) 4654 * ==========================================
[](undefined) 4655 * One of the shortcomings of XXH32 and XXH64 was that their performance was
[](undefined) 4656 * sub-optimal on short lengths. It used an iterative algorithm which strongly
[](undefined) 4657 * favored lengths that were a multiple of 4 or 8.
[](undefined) 4658 *
[](undefined) 4659 * Instead of iterating over individual inputs, we use a set of single shot
[](undefined) 4660 * functions which piece together a range of lengths and operate in constant time.
[](undefined) 4661 *
[](undefined) 4662 * Additionally, the number of multiplies has been significantly reduced. This
[](undefined) 4663 * reduces latency, especially when emulating 64-bit multiplies on 32-bit.
[](undefined) 4664 *
[](undefined) 4665 * Depending on the platform, this may or may not be faster than XXH32, but it
[](undefined) 4666 * is almost guaranteed to be faster than XXH64.
[](undefined) 4667 */
[](undefined) 4668
[](undefined) 4669/*
[](undefined) 4670 * At very short lengths, there isn't enough input to fully hide secrets, or use
[](undefined) 4671 * the entire secret.
[](undefined) 4672 *
[](undefined) 4673 * There is also only a limited amount of mixing we can do before significantly
[](undefined) 4674 * impacting performance.
[](undefined) 4675 *
[](undefined) 4676 * Therefore, we use different sections of the secret and always mix two secret
[](undefined) 4677 * samples with an XOR. This should have no effect on performance on the
[](undefined) 4678 * seedless or withSeed variants because everything _should_ be constant folded
[](undefined) 4679 * by modern compilers.
[](undefined) 4680 *
[](undefined) 4681 * The XOR mixing hides individual parts of the secret and increases entropy.
[](undefined) 4682 *
[](undefined) 4683 * This adds an extra layer of strength for custom secrets.
[](undefined) 4684 */
[](undefined) 4685XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 4686XXH3_len_1to3_64b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 4687{
[](undefined) 4688    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 4689    XXH_ASSERT(1 <= len && len <= 3);
[](undefined) 4690    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 4691    /*
[](undefined) 4692     * len = 1: combined = { input[0], 0x01, input[0], input[0] }
[](undefined) 4693     * len = 2: combined = { input[1], 0x02, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 4694     * len = 3: combined = { input[2], 0x03, input[0], input[1] }
[](undefined) 4695     */
[](undefined) 4696    {   xxh_u8  const c1 = input[0];
[](undefined) 4697        xxh_u8  const c2 = input[len >> 1];
[](undefined) 4698        xxh_u8  const c3 = input[len - 1];
[](undefined) 4699        xxh_u32 const combined = ((xxh_u32)c1 << 16) | ((xxh_u32)c2  << 24)
[](undefined) 4700                               | ((xxh_u32)c3 <<  0) | ((xxh_u32)len << 8);
[](undefined) 4701        xxh_u64 const bitflip = (XXH_readLE32(secret) ^ XXH_readLE32(secret+4)) + seed;
[](undefined) 4702        xxh_u64 const keyed = (xxh_u64)combined ^ bitflip;
[](undefined) 4703        return XXH64_avalanche(keyed);
[](undefined) 4704    }
[](undefined) 4705}
[](undefined) 4706
[](undefined) 4707XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 4708XXH3_len_4to8_64b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)
[](undefined) 4709{
[](undefined) 4710    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 4711    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);
[](undefined) 4712    XXH_ASSERT(4 <= len && len <= 8);
[](undefined) 4713    seed ^= (xxh_u64)XXH_swap32((xxh_u32)seed) << 32;
[](undefined) 4714    {   xxh_u32 const input1 = XXH_readLE32(input);
[](undefined) 4715        xxh_u32 const input2 = XXH_readLE32(input + len - 4);
[](undefined) 4716        xxh_u64 const bitflip = (XXH_readLE64(secret+8) ^ XXH_readLE64(secret+16)) - seed;
[](undefined) 4717        xxh_u64 const input64 = input2 + (((xxh_u64)input1) << 32);
[](undefined) 4718        xxh_u64 const keyed = input64 ^ bitflip;
[](undefined) 4719        return XXH3_rrmxmx(keyed, len);
[](undefined) 4720    }
[](undefined) 4721}
[](undefined) 4722
[](undefined) 4723XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 4724XXH3_len_9to16_64b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)

[](undefined) 4726    XXH_ASSERT(input != NULL);
[](undefined) 4727    XXH_ASSERT(secret != NULL);

[](undefined) 4730        xxh_u64 const bitflip2 = (XXH_readLE64(secret+40) ^ XXH_readLE64(secret+48)) - seed;
[](undefined) 4731        xxh_u64 const input_lo = XXH_readLE64(input)           ^ bitflip1;
[](undefined) 4732        xxh_u64 const input_hi = XXH_readLE64(input + len - 8) ^ bitflip2;
[](undefined) 4733        xxh_u64 const acc = len
[](undefined) 4734                          + XXH_swap64(input_lo) + input_hi

[](undefined) 4740XXH_FORCE_INLINE XXH_PUREF [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603)
[](undefined) 4741XXH3_len_0to16_64b(const xxh_u8* input, size_t len, const xxh_u8* secret, [XXH64_hash_t](/docs/xxhash/v0-8-4/en/02-api/20-group__public/#ga5406b285b18dfcefa93efed489e3b603) seed)

[](undefined) 4748    }
